# Agent notes (nw-tracker)

> Global, project-agnostic rules (fail-fast, provenance, testing, i18n principles,
> commit etiquette, …) live in **GUIDELINES.md**. This file carries the app-specific
> conventions and mechanics. History (why a rule exists, what changed when) lives in
> `docs/history/AGENTS-2026-11-12.md`; this file states the current rules.

## Language and i18n

- **UI copy via i18n (es + en)**: all user-facing strings live in **`client/src/i18n/master.json`** — the single source of truth, nested like the locale files, every leaf holding both languages: `{ "en": "Save", "es": "Guardar" }`. `locales/es.json` / `locales/en.json` are **generated** by `npm run i18n:generate -w nw-tracker-client` (`client/scripts/i18n-generate.mjs`); never hand-edit them — `client/src/i18n/localeSync.test.ts` fails on drift. The master stays nested because some leaf keys contain literal dots (e.g. `panelAccounts.addAccount."accountType.equity"`). No hardcoded Spanish or English labels in components except via `t('…')` / `i18n.t('…')`.
- **Translation keys in English**: dotted paths such as `dashboard.cards.netWorth`, `brokerage.groups.mutual_funds`. Code identifiers use English slugs (`mutual_funds`, `real_estate`); display copy lives in `master.json`.
- **Shared labels**: use helpers in `client/src/i18n/index.ts` (e.g. `ccExpenseCategoryLabel`, `flowsPlBucketLabel`; a nav node's label is the server's, resolved with `resolveNavTreeLabel`) so sidebar, cards and charts read the same keys.
- When adding UI text, add the key (both languages) to `master.json` first, run `npm run i18n:generate -w nw-tracker-client`, then reference it. Migrate hardcoded strings when touching an area.
- **UI language preference**: `client/src/languagePreference.ts` (localStorage `nw-tracker.language`), seeded once from `navigator.language` (Spanish → `es`, else `en`; non-browser/vitest contexts stay `es` so label assertions are deterministic), then the stored value is authoritative. Changed from `/panel/settings` via `DisplayPreferencesContext.setLanguage` (calls `i18n.changeLanguage`, persists, syncs `<html lang>`; re-renders `AppTree` top-down). The decimal separator is an independent setting. **Do not cache `t()` results** in `useMemo`/state without the language in the deps — translate at render time.
- **Dates**: numeric dates render as ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` (timestamps `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`) in every language — never `dd-mm-yy` / `toLocale*` short styles. Month-name labels (`dic 2026` ↔ `Dec 2026`, ticks `dic 16` ↔ `Dec 16`) go through `client/src/formatDateLabel.ts`, which reads `i18n.language` at call time — same no-caching rule as `t()`.
- **Enforced by `npm run check:conventions`** (root; also runs at the end of root `npm run typecheck`): flags accented Spanish in client string literals/JSX (comments exempt), `Intl.NumberFormat` outside `format.ts`, hardcoded-locale `toLocaleString` without date options, month-name tables outside `formatDateLabel.ts`, `isFetching` / `isRefetching` in `client/src/components` / `pages` (see «Loading states»), and ticker-suffix parsing (`.endsWith(".SN")`, `lastIndexOf(".")` on a ticker, `\.SN$` regexes, `LIKE '%.SN'`) in `server/src` / `client/src` / `ingest/src` outside `server/src/priceProviders/` (see «Instrument registry and price providers»). It also flags currency hardcoding (docs/any-currency-plan.md): `formatClp` / `formatUsd` (and `formatClpUfDay`, `formatUsdFine`) in `client/src/components` / `pages`, a unit or currency compared with a literal code, fixed currency lists and `"clp" | "usd"` unions outside `rates.ts` / `valueConvert.ts` / `displayUnits.ts`, and `_clp` / `_usd` / `_eur` keys in `server/contracts/src/dto`; every hit fails unless its line says why the currency is native to its source (`// convention-ok: <reason>`: an F22 figure is pesos by law, a Santander feed has a peso and a dollar side, a card's billing frame is pesos), and a migration above 312 with `CHECK (… IN ('clp' …))` fails outright (reference `currencies(code)`). There is no ratchet baseline. «Is this pesos?» is `isHomeCurrency` (`HOME_CURRENCY` in the contracts' `dto/apiConstants.ts`, re-exported by the server's `displayUnits.ts`); a client figure one currency by nature formats through its named wrapper in `client/src/formatNative.ts` (`formatF22Pesos`, `formatCardBillingPesos`, `formatBillPesos`, …), a display-unit value through `formatMoney` / `formatCurrency`. Escape hatch: `// convention-ok: <reason>` on the line.
- **Dependency guard, also in `npm run check:conventions`** (`scripts/check-deps.mjs`, baseline `scripts/check-deps.baseline.json`). Builds the TypeScript import graph of `server/src`, `client/src`, `ingest/src` and `server/contracts/src` (tests excluded, type-only imports counted, `.js` resolved to `.ts`/`.tsx`, `nw-tracker-contracts` to its `index.ts`) and fails when a file joins an import cycle the baseline does not list, when a new edge crosses a forbidden boundary (contracts → server/client/ingest, client ↔ server, ingest ↔ server: `server/contracts` is the only shared code), or when `cacheRegistry.ts` imports a module it did not. Prints the largest cycle, the edge count vs the baseline and the top fan-in, fan-out and transitive-reach files. Cycles may only shrink: after a change that shrinks them, `node scripts/check-deps.mjs --write-baseline` locks it in (docs/untangle-plan.md F1). Shared formats (numbers, dd/mm and ISO days, day arithmetic, year-months and Spanish month names, accent folding, RUT and account numbers, merchant text, the zod building blocks) live in `server/contracts/src/formats/`, each rule pinned by a case file in `server/contracts/data/` (`npm run test -w nw-tracker-contracts`); the Python parsers assert the same files.
- **Number formatting**: all numbers (CLP, USD, UF, units, percents) share one decimal-separator convention from `client/src/numberFormatPreference.ts` (localStorage `nw-tracker.decimalSeparator`, seeded from the IANA timezone; toolbar toggle overrides). Always format via `client/src/format.ts` helpers — never `Intl.NumberFormat` / `toLocaleString` with a hardcoded locale for numbers. A separator change re-renders (not remounts) via `AppTree`; **do not cache formatted strings** in `useMemo`/state unless `decimalSeparator` is in the deps — memoize raw numbers, format at render. **Typed numbers:** every number field reads through `parseNumberInput` / `parseNumberInputOrThrow` (format.ts). A number that fits one convention is read that way («1500,5», «1.500.000», «1,234.56»); only a single mark followed by exactly three digits («1.500») is decided by the setting (1500 with comma decimals, 1.5 with period decimals); anything else is rejected with `common.numberInvalid`; forms keep their own sign/range/integer rules. Prefills and placeholders use `formatNumberInput`. Never `Number()` / `parseFloat` / `.replace(/\./g, "")` on typed text, and no `type="number"` inputs (the browser parses those by its own locale). The flows amount filter (`flowsAmountFilter.ts`) also takes an amount copied from the table: a leading `$` / `US$` and the negative «($500)».

## Fail fast + provenance — app specifics

The fail-fast/no-fallbacks and free-text-is-provenance rules live in **GUIDELINES.md**. App-specific instantiations:

- Existing docs follow the no-fallback rule (e.g. CC matrix month = `period_to` only, no filename/statement-date guesses) — extend it to new code.
- **Notes are provenance:** movement/account `note` text is for humans; runtime never branches on note substrings and never reads `cfraser/` files on request paths. Structured homes for machine state: `movements.flow_kind` (deposit/mortgage/brokerage kinds), `loan_payments` + the property's UF `valuations`, `movement_mirror_merges`, `accounts.import_key`, `credit_card_account_config.card_last4` / `.nav_retired` / `.superseded_target_last4`.

## Personal data pipeline (cfraser) — see PARSERS.md

Docs for the personal import pipeline — credit-card PDF parsing quirks, Fintual
certificado, AFP UNO certificates — live in **untracked `PARSERS.md`**
(gitignored, imported by CLAUDE.md; main checkout only, like the `cfraser/` data it
documents). If missing, restore from `~/Documents/backups/nw-tracker/`. The excel/CSV
reconstruction pipeline is retired — the DB plus snapshots are the single source of
truth; new movements are entered manually or via the document importers below.

## CC chart / Pasivos convention

**"owed on that date", not billing-month totals.** Per-card chart lines, the Pasivos Total, `accountMarkClpAtYmd` and the dashboard Pasivos line read stored `valuations` via `latestCreditCardValuationRowAsOf`. **Every date, today included, is walked:** `last anchor strictly before it + evidence dated after it` (`ccOwedWalkClpAtYmd` in `ccOwedWalk.ts`, the choke point `latestCreditCardDisplayedBalance` uses; `as_of_date` returns the walked-through date so callers don't re-apply the window). Never value today with the live billing formula (facturado + cupo en cuotas − cuota a pagar next mes): it is a different framing of the same debt and dumps the frame gap into today's delta. Today's stamp is written from the walk, so tomorrow's anchor shares its frame. Billing surfaces (Balance total, detalle por mes, cupo) keep the live formula. No separate "Saldo pasivos" line (Total carries it). Month-end points written by `upsertCreditCardValuationsFromLedger` = billing-detail `balance_total` = **facturado + post-close plan remainder** (`installmentRemainingClpByCalendarMonth`, cuotas due strictly after the month) + post-cierre activity (charges +, PAGOs −), re-synced after web-paste repairs in `mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows`. Don't subtract «cuota a pagar next mes» from an anchor: that only nets a double count in the bank's live cupo frame and sinks closed anchors one cuota low. Re-stamp: `server/scripts/restamp-cc-anchor-frame.ts`. **Today-stamps self-purge on contradicted evidence:** every CC write also stamps `today`, valid only until dated evidence for an earlier day arrives, so the writer takes **`affectedEvidenceFromYmd`** and deletes every non-month-end stamp from that date through yesterday before rewriting (the purge runs before the has-points early return). **Every caller that adds or removes dated evidence passes it**: the merge import (`earliestInsertedTxDate` over genuinely inserted lines — dedupe skips contribute nothing, so re-pasting is idempotent — plus new `purchase_date`s and the header pagado date), line deletions (`earliestTransactionDateForLineIds`, read BEFORE deleting), manual installment create/edit/delete (edits use min(old, new)). Pure recomputes omit it.

- **A card's P/L is its cost of financing, loss-negative.** Liabilities mark as positive debt; every P/L surface uses `pl = prior − owed − flow`. Card P/L = **−(section-3 charges: intereses, comisiones, impuestos)** on the day charged (`ccFinancingCostDaily.ts`, classifier `isClp/UsdSection3FinancingChargeMerchant`, held by `ccFinancingIdentity.test.ts`). Everything else is **flow, derived** (`flow = −Δowed + financing`), never summed from events, because cierre re-splits of the anchors are measurement noise; ordinary days read `pl = 0`. **Installment interest** (`ccInstallmentInterest.ts`): for a plan printed with rate > 0, interest = Σ printed cuotas − printed principal, dated on the purchase date (the owed walk adds the whole total then, so flow stays at the principal); a 0% plan rounding a few pesos over its principal, or a plan no statement has printed yet, has none. The dashboard's financing summary reads it in the purchase's billing month. **In «deuda en cuotas» interest bills cuota by cuota** (`ccInstallmentInterestBillingEvents`): principal on the purchase date, each cuota's interest share at the close that bills it (projected close if unbilled); retirement removes whole cuotas. The owed walk and P/L keep the whole total on the purchase date. **The fx move on unpaid foreign debt is P/L** (`ccRevaluationCostByDate`, see «Card dollars in pesos»). Card flows are not personal capital: out of `/flows/deposits`, the deposits reconciliation and the "aportes acum." companions; the `pago_tarjeta` mirror's card leg emits nothing (`accountDeposits.ts`; the cash leg emits its withdrawal). The mortgage's daily flow leg is `mortgageSheetPaymentEventsThroughDate` (depto ledger).
- **The installment schedule is facturación/statement months everywhere.** `purchaseFirstDueYm` + index = the month each cuota **bills**; plan sums per month equal the statements' billed cuotas; `cuotaAPagarNextMesClp` / facturaciones read the billing month itself (never +1). First-due precedence: cuota-01 statement month → cuota-00 preamble's statement month + 1 → stored `first_due_month` (web-paste nudge, write-once) → manual guess = the purchase's own cycle (`billingMonthContainingPurchase`). **Manual anchors with no statement cuota evidence clamp to `last fully-closed month + 1`** (an imported statement that didn't bill the plan is negative evidence; statement-driven, never calendar). **A facturación closes only when every statement currency the card's history carries is imported** (`lastPdfBillingMonthForAccount` / `accountRequiresUsdStatementClose`): a lone USD twin must not advance `open_billing_month` or retarget pastes. `reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfClose` settles the bucket per currency as each twin arrives (keeping the whole bucket would double-count). A dormant never-billed USD side closes on CLP alone, and a USD twin still missing once the bank has closed a LATER facturación (later statement, feed-observed close, or passed announced close) is not coming — the month closes on CLP alone (`pdfClosedBillingMonthsForAccount`); the bucket reconcile keeps the stricter `hasEveryStatementTwinForBillingMonth`, so a CLP-only month's dollar lines wait for a USD statement. A card that stopped billing (`cardStoppedBilling`, `cards/lifecycle.ts`) gets no provisional rows. Web-paste plans carry the issuer card_group (`'santander'`/`'BCI'`), PDF ones the parser groups (`'A'`/`'B'`/`'INTL'`); the manual↔statement twin reconcile treats the web-paste group as compatible with any PDF group and tolerates ±2 days on indexed cuota lines (exact merchant/amount/cuotas required). Nota-cancelled plans (full-principal NOTA DE CREDITO matched by posting **statement month** — banks backdate transaction dates — or a strictly-later transaction date) are excluded from every schedule aggregate via `schedulePurchases`. A settled ledger doesn't force-active a card (`cardStoppedBilling` needs outstanding principal, else the tail heuristic).
- **Cuotas por facturación (`payload.months`)**: one row per facturación — `month`, `pay_by_date` (printed PAGAR HASTA when closed, derived for open/projected), `total_clp`, `debt_after_clp` (suffix sum; last 0). Rows roll off once the pay-by passes (Chile day); `totals.next_calendar_month` = the **pay** month of the next payment. Purchase tables show `purchase_date` + `purchase_billing_month` (`billingMonthContainingPurchase`).
- **Facturación proxy earnings are keyed by the facturación that billed the money** (`ccInvestmentProxy.ts`, column `facturacion_proxy` by `billing_month`): each purchase floats from its date to that facturación's pay-by. A printed cuota is keyed by `first_due_month` + N − 1 and withdrawn on its payment row's `pay_by_date`; a one-shot line by `facturacionMonthByStatementDate` (stale bucket → open month) and withdrawn on that pay-by (`facturacionPayByIsoResolver`), so open/provisional paste lines count. Superseded lines and count-less cuota purchases are out; an unparseable line date throws.
- **Months after the open facturación are plan-only, pay-frame projections** (`projected: true`, `appendProjectedBillingDetailRows`): facturado null; saldo/cupo at month-end M = plan remainder after (M−1)'s close (cuotas leave the debt on their pay-by, not their close). One step per month, flat from today through the open month-end, plus a trailing zero month. Identity: chart month-end M = calendar row M's cuota + `debt_after_clp`. Closed months keep the statement frame; the seam is smooth because bank-frame(M) ≡ pay-frame(M+1). `latestBillingDetailRow` skips projected rows. **The historial chart's two lines are the daily walks sampled** (`ccHistorialLinesAtMonthEndsClp` → `linesForMonths` of `buildCcHistorialChartSeries`, from the card payload and `creditCardGroupLedgerResponse` over the daily block's masters): «saldo total» (owed walk) and «deuda en cuotas» (plan-debt walk) at each month's last day, today for the current month, the plan tail after; bars per facturación. The detalle's open-month roll-forward adds plans bought after the closed month's calendar month-end (`installmentPurchasesClpAfter`); its «cupo en cuotas» keeps the statement frame (facturado + cupo = balance).
- **Historial bars: one stacked bar per facturación** (`buildCcHistorialChartSeries`), bottom to top «Facturado CLP» (CLP facturado − cuotas, net of notas, below zero when negative via `stackOffset="sign"`), «Facturado CLP (cuotas)» (`cuota_a_pagar_clp`) and «Facturado US$» (pesos at the paid rate; tooltip «US$ X ($Y)»), one amber hue, cuotas darkest; tooltip top to bottom (saldo total, deuda en cuotas, facturado over segments). A projection-only month carries just its cuotas; an open month with no lines yet splits its detail facturado as CLP. **A group's bar is Σ its cards' bars** (`memberSeries`), and the merged billing-detail row adds a projected member's cuota to a month another member billed (`mergedTotalFacturadoClp`, «≈ cuota»); never re-split bars from merged facturaciones (they miss the projected member's cuotas). **Day mode: one stack per billing month** (`facturacionBarsOnMonthCloseDates`, same cached bundle): Σ of members' bars, dated on the latest close among members that billed it (`closeEvidenceForBillingMonth` for projected); a member with no bar lends neither pesos nor close. The client extends the grid past the plan tail to a later close, draws fixed 6 px segments (Recharts shrinks `barSize` to sub-pixel day bands) and lists bars within `athTooltipIndexTolerance` rows. No «Coste financiero vs facturado» chart, no `extraOffsets`; stale `nw-credit-card-extra-offsets:<id>` keys are swept at boot (`DisplayPreferencesContext`).

- **Card foreign debt: values follow the date's rate, costs keep the paid rate** (`ccOwedWalk.ts`, migration 259, `docs/holdings-cards-plan.md`). A card owes a sub-balance per currency it bills in (`CcOwedNative`, map by currency, pesos always; test issuer `euronova`, `ccEuroCardOwed.test.ts`); its value on a date (owed walk, anchors, Pasivos, cash netting, detalle month frame, daily CC block) converts each at that date's rate with `convertCurrency` (debt side; a currency in credit at the asset side; live today). Evidence stays in the bank's currency (`normalizedPostCloseLines` carry `currency` + `amount`; a third-currency line's pesos at the debt side of its facturación's debt-frame date). **Statement lines store `amount` + `currency` + `cuota_amount`** (migration 319; a line's currency is its own — an open bucket is one peso statement holding dollar lines; no `amount_clp`/`amount_usd`/`valor_cuota_mensual_*` columns, dropped by 324). The peso/dollar view is `cards/lineAmountSql.ts` (`lineAmountClpSql` / `lineAmountUsdSql` / `lineCuotaClpSql` / `lineCuotaUsdSql`, `lineLegacyAmounts`: peso line = pesos + NULL dollars; dollar line = 0 pesos + dollars; other currency NULL in all four); kind `cc_line_kind_native` (`cardLineKind276.ts`); the import fingerprint hashes the legacy shape; `legacyAmountColumn` (`cards/statementStorage.ts`) is the one mapping. Anchors are per currency in `cc_owed_anchors`; month-end anchors split the statement frame by currency via `ccStatementOwedByCurrencyByMonth`, computed for every foreign currency the card bills (`cardForeignCurrencies`, `ccFacturacionForeignRate.ts`) at uniform rate 1000 and per currency at 900/1100 (`withUniformDebtRates`, `debtFrameClpPerUnit` in `ccExpenseAmountClp.ts`), throwing unless affine in each rate; `valuations` keeps one CLP row per anchor date at that date's rate (group charts, liabilities overview). A traspaso de deuda moves foreign out and pesos in on the same day. **Fx move is P/L (user decision):** `ccFinancingCostByDateInUnit(card, unit)` (`ccFinancingCostDaily.ts`) = section-3 charges + plan interest (pesos: `ccSection3FinancingClpByDate`; other units: each charge at its own day's debt-side rate) + `ccRevaluationCostByDate(card, unit)` (each sub-balance in another currency than the unit × the day's rate change: in pesos, foreign owed at previous day-end × Δ buy rate; in dollars, pesos owed × Δ sell rate). **A foreign payment counts at the pesos actually paid (user decision)** where its real rate is on file (`foreignDebtPaymentsForAccount(card, currency)`: paired divisas purchase, a traspaso's peso leg, the traced cost from a foreign cash account, `foreignCashCostLots.ts`): the payment day adds `pesos paid − foreign paid × day rate`, so its fx P/L is `foreign × (real rate − previous day's debt rate)` and its flow the pesos paid; a traspaso's step against the buy rate is fx P/L with zero net flow. No real rate on file → the day's rate. Derived flow `−Δowed + cost` in the unit (daily `ccDerivedFlows`; monthly `ccFinancingCostBetweenInUnit`; dashboard day cell), P/L = −cost = Δvalue − flow, days sum to months. For a card in USD read `ccOwedByCurrencyAtYmd`. The statement frame (`balance_statement_clp`: Cupo card's used figure, stored snapshot) converts at pay-by − 1 (`balanceUsdFxDateIso`). Re-stamp: `server/scripts/restamp-cc-owed-anchors.ts [--apply]`. **What a facturación shows it cost** (row's foreign pesos, historial bars, its expense lines) is the paid rate: the payments after its close up to the next close, weighted — divisas purchases (`pago_tarjeta` with a USD counter leg) and traspasos (`cc_traspaso_deuda_links`); today's rate (`fxForLiveMtm`) while unpaid and the pay-by (inclusive) is ahead; the pay-by − 1 debt rate once the pay-by passed with no payment on file. **Per foreign currency** (`ccFacturacionForeignRate.ts`): rows carry `facturado_foreign`, one entry per billed currency (`{currency, amount, clp, rate_clp, rate_source}`; `amount` null on a closed month with no statement, 0 on an open cycle that billed none; a third currency's closed statement bills its header, else its lines, `foreignBilledFromStatementLines`); non-dollar pay-by/live rates from `rates.ts` (debt side), dollars `fx_daily` / `fxForLiveMtm`. `rate_source` is `paid` | `live` | `pay_by` (a merged group row names one only when members agree); expense lines read the same cached rows (`facturacionForeignRateWithSourceResolver(card, currency, rows)`) so the modal adds up to the row; the historial foreign segment is `facturado_foreign_clp` (label «Facturado en moneda extranjera»). Lines are read in their own currency (`cardLineNativeAmount` in `ccExpenseAmountClp.ts`). A foreign payment line (`isCcForeignPaymentLine`: ABONO DE DIVISAS, PAGO / MONTO CANCELADO in that currency, a traspaso's foreign leg) shows the pesos actually paid (`foreignPaymentLineClpResolver(card, currency, …)`): its paired divisas purchase (via the payment evidence key, see «Checking↔CC payment mirrors»), a traspaso's CLP leg, else the rate of the facturación whose (close, next close] window holds it (`facturacionPaidOn`). The row = the modal's Gastos + Coste financiero; Abonos pay the previous facturación. **Coste financiero is the server's call** (`markFinancingCostLines` in `flowsExpenses.ts`: `is_financing_cost` on positive one-shot section-3 lines per `ccStatementLineRules.json`); never keep a client copy of the rules. Import dedupe keeps the statement-date fx (compares amounts only).
- **A card payment in a foreign currency counts at the pesos that currency cost** (`foreignCashCostLots.ts`, migration 231). A `pago_tarjeta` from a USD cash account (`currency = 'usd'`, no counter leg) enters the paid rate (`usdDebtPaymentsForAccount`, dollar-only) at a traced cost (`foreignOutflowPesoCostByMovement`, per currency; a conversion from another foreign currency carries its slices' pesos; `market` lots at the stored mid via `rates.ts`, dollars at the `fx_daily` close): lots walked FIFO through the cash accounts in scope (the source plus, recursively, every same-currency cash account that funded one in scope) — `purchase` (a CLP→USD transfer's pesos), `own_transfer` (pesos the source outflow consumed), `market` (other inflows at `fxRowOnOrBefore`; no rate throws). A shortfall above a cent throws, as does a dollar payment from a non-USD-cash account or one the tracer didn't price. **Broker withdrawals reserve at request time:** each `broker_withdrawal_requests` row takes its gross FIFO at `requested_at`; its booked transfer (`incoming_wire_bookings`) carries all reserved pesos for the net, the fee draws the rest at 0; a booked reservation must end empty and a booking whose transfer was deleted throws. Order: Chile date, then time when all are timed, else arrivals first then id (a buy and its funding wire may be written in either order), requests among their account's events — a request's day must have times on every other event of its account, else throw. Times live in **`movement_event_times`** (migration 231: movement → ISO UTC `occurred_at`, `source = 'broker_mail'`, message id), stamped by the Fintual branch of `broker.notifications` for every row it writes, promotes or matches (so re-sending staged mails stamps existing movements); never overwritten (`event_times_stamped` in details).

- **Facturado financing links (`cc_facturado_financing_links`, «Financiamiento de facturado en cuotas»).** A facturado of one card paid by installment purchases on another is a link; `applyCcFacturadoFinancingProjection` (`ccFacturadoFinancingProjectionLines.ts`, last step of the expenses payload) hides the financing cuotas (`excluded`), keeps financed purchases in their month in «Total» mode and in «Por cuota» splits each equally over the payment's distinct cuota billing months as `split_only` lines dated the 28th with `installment_total_clp` (for the «/$total» superscript). **Financing cuotas match by exact `purchase_key`**, so statement cuota lines must carry the same with-total `installment-h:` key as their plan-derived siblings: `installmentLedgerTotalForStatementLine` (`ccExpenseCategories.ts`, behind both the flows `installment_total_clp` stamp and `resolveCcExpensePurchaseKey`) resolves via the line's `cc_installment_payments.parser_row_id`, then identity when matching plans share one total (twins), then the printed cuota among same-identity plans, else the legacy no-total key (still resolved by category/notes readers). Identity-only lookup breaks on twins with different totals. **Interest gap = Σ cuotas − the closed statement's `monto_facturado`** (`statementFacturadoClpForBillingMonth`: CLP, non-web-paste, by statement month; identical duplicate copies collapse, disagreeing totals throw; no closed statement → no gap line; ≤1 peso per cuota is rounding) — never Σ cuotas − Σ financed lines (that reads the financed card's own cuotas and notas as interest). A facturado paid as a one-shot needs no link: `no_cuenta` keeps it out of gastos.

- **Financing links declare themselves** (`ccFacturadoFinancingAuto.ts`, migration 297 `cc_facturado_payment_charges`). After every card write (`mergeCcAccountFromParsedRows`, feed import end, a plan created by hand or from a tagged line) one matcher looks for an unlinked closed CLP facturado paid from another card master. Pool: every peso purchase there — cuota plans (at principal: printed for interest plans, else total), feed lines tagged as cuota purchases awaiting a plan, one-shots (`card_purchase`, no installment flag) — each once by flow, dated after the close and up to 10 days past the pay-by, and only where the merchant opens like a place the paid card's issuer is paid at (`facturado_paid_at_merchant_prefixes` on `CC_ISSUERS`: BCI Lider «RECAUDACION», «EXPRESS», «EXP LIDER», «HIP LIDER», «HIPER LIDER», «LIDER»; none for Santander and CMR, so theirs aren't matched). Per paying card and day, combinations summing to the facturado to the peso; **fewest payments win**; interchangeable twins (same card, day, kinds, merchants, amounts) take the lowest ids; any other tie, a purchase fitting two facturados, or a day with >10 candidates writes nothing. Existing links are never touched. **A match is recorded once** — the link row, plans as `finances_facturado` flow links (`upsertCcFacturadoFinancingLink`), one-shots as `cc_facturado_payment_charges` by flow (flow merges move them) — and never re-matched; a plan-less record whose charges no longer hold a one-shot line is dropped and the month re-matched. A match with a tagged line waits for its plan before recording; its PAGO does not wait. Each one-shot charge is filed as an internal transfer («Único» row) unless the line already has an «Único» row, a per-line override, a declined automatic category, or a general rule giving «no cuenta» / internal transfer. **PAGO planting:** if the paid card shows no payment of that amount (payment lines −3…+5 days, or a statement header naming it), a PAGO is planted in its open bucket (batch kind `cc_financing_payment`, registered in `cc_planted_payment_lines`, so the bank's own PAGO in the Lider listing replaces it), and the real statement PAGO settles it. **Partly financed facturado** (charges + plans): only the financed share (facturado − Σ charges) ÷ facturado of each financed purchase spreads over the cuota months, the rest stays in its month (`financing-proj-rest:` split_only lines; a mortgage line's carrying and amortization split likewise); interest = Σ cuotas − financed principal. A hand-made link replaces the record's charges; the manager lists only records with plans. Near misses (recent facturado, no payment, no match, one day's issuer-place charges ≥ half of it) are reported in the plan, never written.
- **Facturación closes come from the bank's evidence, not the config cycle** (migration 185, `ccBillingCloses.ts`). **Announced:** statements print the next cycle (Santander «PRÓXIMO PERÍODO DE FACTURACIÓN …», BCI «Próximo Período de Facturación …») → `cc_statements.next_period_from` / `next_period_to` (`pdf_next_period_*` in `cc_statement_reconcile.py`); the Santander JSON's FechaProxFact fills `next_period_to` (for a PDF-owned close the JSON importer fills it when missing and reports disagreement). Period end vs printed next start gives the issuer's **close-day rule** (`closeDayOffsetDays`): Santander starts the next cycle ON the close day (a close-day purchase bills next month), BCI the day after. **Observed:** the Santander unbilled feed opens with a per-currency **SALDO INICIAL** row dated at the latest close, valued at that facturación's «Monto total facturado»; the scraper keeps it (`saldoInicial` per slide) and `import:santander-movements` records it in `cc_feed_billing_closes` (a repeat changing date or amount throws). Precedence: statement → feed → announced → estimate (`closeEvidenceForBillingMonth`); **the estimate carries the account's latest known close day into the month** (clamped); the config cycle estimates only for a card with no close on record. Never «fix» the config cycle to the real close day: it is the open buckets' `statement_date` identity (`statementCloseDdMmYyyyForBillingMonth`) and must not collide with an incoming statement's date. **The open month advances on the bank's close** (`lastClosedBillingMonthForAccount` = latest statement close, a later feed close, or a later announced close whose next cycle has started); months between the last statement and that close are **provisionally closed** (`provisionallyClosedBillingMonthsForAccount`): facturado = feed SALDO INICIAL (USD at the pay-by) when observed, else the app's estimate; the billing-detail row is statement-framed (`as_of_kind: statement`, as_of = the real close, `provisional: true`); facturaciones carry `is_provisional_close`, `close_date_source` and, with the bank's figure, `provisional_estimate_total_clp`. The client marks a provisional month with a bare asterisk (same as projected; the hover hint differs), an estimated close «≈», an unprinted pay-by «≈» (`pay_by_source`: `statement` | `derived` | null). **The open month's cupo subtracts what a provisional close billed** (`provisionalBilledCuotasClp` in `cupoEnCuotasForBillingMonth`), since the live cupo marks cuotas billed only once a statement prints them. A provisional close is **not negative schedule evidence**: the manual-plan clamp reads `lastPdfBillingMonthForAccount`. **Open buckets keep their config `statement_date`** (`cc_statements` is unique on source + date and sums group by statement date); displayed closes come from `closeDateForBillingMonth`. **Feed routing:** a post-close feed row goes to the later of the month after the observed close (`rows_billing_month`) and its own cycle (`feedLineBillingMonths` in `cardUnbilledMovementsApply.ts` via `billingMonthContainingPurchase`; one bucket per month through `lineBillingMonths` on `ccWebPasteToCsvRecords`; hand paste unchanged) — needed because the bank rolls its list the night after the close day. Bucket lines under earlier months whose one-shot key the feed still lists move forward (`moveOpenBucketLinesByDedupeKey`, once per target month). The repair pass moves lines from the next cycle's first day (`nextPeriodStartIsoForBillingMonth`) and creates the open bucket only when a line moves in (an eager create leaves retired cards an empty open facturación each month). **Statement reconcile matches, never deletes a date window** (`reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfClose`): a bucket line on the statement (same merchant, amount, date ±1 day, `webPasteLineMatchesStatementLine`) is deleted; an unmatched line inside the billed cycle (up to, excluding, the next cycle's first day) is deleted as a differently-settled pre-auth when an unused statement line of the same currency within ±4 days has a matching merchant or the same amount, and otherwise kept as an annulled purchase (see «Annulled card purchases»); an unmatched line outside moves to the open facturación; a later bucket's line goes only when the statement bills it. **Stale buckets** carried into the open month are only those of statement-closed months. **One rule assigns a statement's lines to a facturación:** `facturacionMonthByStatementDate` (own month; a stale bucket's → open month); open-month facturado/payment sums use it via `statementDatesForFacturacion`; expense lines' `billing_month` is stamped from it with a `web_paste` flag for lines the modal may delete; the facturación modal is a plain filter (`flowLinesForFacturacionMonth`). Never keep a client copy of the rule. **Cross-check:** a statement for a feed-observed close must print the same date and bill exactly the SALDO INICIAL (CLP to the peso, USD to the cent) — `assertFeedClosesMatchStatements` throws in every card merge and feed import. A pasted «SALDO INICIAL» row is skipped (`skipped_saldo_inicial`).

- **A derived pay-by follows the issuer** (`CcDerivedPayByRule` on `CC_ISSUERS`, `derivedPayByIso` in `ccInstallmentPayBy.ts`, `ccDerivedPayByRuleForAccount` in `ccIssuerForAccount.ts`). No document announces the next PAGAR HASTA (the Santander JSON's `FechaVenc` is the current statement's own), so open, provisional and projected pay-bys derive from the close: **BCI** the 10th of the next month as it falls, weekends included; **Santander** the 10th moved back to the previous Chile business day (sometimes a day late vs print, never early); **CMR** close + 16 days. Issuer = `credit_card_account_config.issuer` (`ccIssuerSlugForMasterAccount`; no config row or an unlisted issuer throws). All readers go through it: facturaciones rows and the expense-line / proxy resolvers, the debt fx date (`balanceUsdFxDateIso`), the plan ledger's open and projected months (`closeDateForBillingMonth`), and the import fallback for a statement with no printed pay-by. `marketHolidays.ts` must cover the years a forward estimate needs. Derived rows read «≈» (`pay_by_source` = `derived`).
- **A dollar statement with no printed total bills its own lines** (`ccUsdStatementBilled.ts`; the Santander international JSON header is all null): `usdBilledFromStatementLines` = charges and credits net, previous facturación's payments (`isCcUsdPaymentLine`) excluded; `feedCloseStatementMismatches` holds it to the feed's dollar SALDO INICIAL.
- **«Tarjetas: próximos pagos»** (`dashboardCcUpcomingPayments.ts`, `UpcomingPaymentsPanel.tsx`). Per active card master (`cardStoppedBilling`; superseded masters already out of `listCreditCardMasterAccountIds`), two rows of the card page's facturaciones (`billingDetailCacheForAccount(...).facturaciones`, never rebuilt): the open one (latest `is_open_month`; a card with no close reads every month open) and the latest before it, each with what it still OWES, per currency (`CcCardPayments.paid` / `open_credits` are maps by currency). The closed one owes its amount less payments after its close (pesos: payment lines and headers via `listCcPaymentEvidenceRows`, one per key — a planted PAGO counts the day paid; foreign: `foreignDebtPaymentsForAccount`) and less the open cycle's credits (`openFacturacionCredits`: notas/refunds, applied first by the bank); the open one gets back credits the closed one used and loses what payments and credits put above the closed amount (overpayment carries). Only the latest closed one can owe. Foreign owed at today's mid (`rates.ts`, live for dollars while the fx day is open). Rows owing nothing are omitted (when paid, not at pay-by); a closed one still owing past its pay-by carries `overdue` and counts in the current month until the next close. Rows carry `amount_clp` (owed), `billed_clp` (`facturado_total_clp`, shown «de $X» once partly paid), ISO close with `close_observed` (statement/feed ✓; announced/estimated ≈), the pay-by with `pay_by_source`, open/provisional flags. Grouped by due month in the block's `months` (pay month, total, payments in card order; overdue → current month), one card per month in a `.cards` block like the «Flujos» period cards. Always shown: empty reads «Nothing due in the coming months»; a placeholder bundle (null = unknown) with no stored copy shows it dimmed and empty. Pesos in every display unit. A BCI payment counts once a card line shows it (statement or `cc:manual-payment`; no feed or receipt mail).
- **«Próximos pagos» covers every payment due** (`upcomingPayments.ts`). The bundle's `upcoming_payments` groups items by due month over `UPCOMING_PAYMENTS_HORIZON_MONTHS` (6); the panel shows two months, «Show more» the rest. Sources: cards (above); the **mortgage**, every cuota from the next unpaid, on its best UF day (`buildMortgageUfReminder`, else the 11th), min (amortization projection, `buildDeptoPaymentScenarioRows`) to max (bank's posted máxima) in pesos at that day's UF; **gastos comunes** per place with `hoa` statements, every month (bill of month B paid in B + 1 by the day the latest statement named, at its amount until that month's statement arrives), paid when a bill row covers the month or the statement is settled; **contribuciones** per owned property, cuotas due the last day of April, June, September, November, as many paid as the year has bill rows, amount from the first paid one, else last year's same cuota UF-readjusted; **declared payments** (`recurring_payments`, migrations 254/264/267, `GET|POST /api/upcoming-payments/recurring`, `DELETE …/:id`; matching pure in `recurringPaymentMatch.ts`, loading in `upcomingPayments.ts`). An entry is **manual** (`manual = 1`, listed until a line pays it) or **automatic** (card-charged, never listed; occurrences in `automatic_recurring` with status paid / overdue / upcoming and the paying line); **yearly** (month + day) or **monthly** (day; `due_month` null); amount in pesos or UF (`currency`; UF of the due day, projected past the last published, estimated). Lines match by **patterns** (`recurring_payment_patterns`, case/accent-insensitive, merchant or shown name) from `match_days_before` (14) before to `match_days_after` (7) after the due date and, with `amount_tolerance_pct`, within that % of the expected pesos (UF entry: amount × the UF of the line's day or any of the five after — a pending listing is priced at its posting day; peso entry: its placeholder). A peso occurrence's placeholder is the latest matched payment of the entry or of a sibling (same name and frequency) earlier the same calendar year, else the stated amount; estimated («≈») until paid. **A payment whose document states what it covers** (vehicle permit expiry, `RecurringLine.covers_through` from `payment_receipt.vehicle_permit`) is that payment whatever its amount, and later occurrences due before the expiry are `covered` (a first cuota expires on the second's due day, so never covers it). Occurrences settle in due-date order from 400 days before declaration; a line pays at most one, the nearest; an occurrence before declaration is never asked for. **Pending listings under another name:** some automatic charges list first under one name and post days later under another at the same pesos (e.g. «CONVENIO P.A.T.» → «SEG AUTO SANTANDER»); both are patterns of one entry. `withRecurringPaymentNames` (`recurringLineNames.ts`, after the receipts) tags fitting card lines (`automaticRecurringMatchForLine`) with `recurring_payment` (name, patterns); `expenseLineDisplayNames` shows such a line under the vendor catalog's name for the entry's first other pattern its merchant lacks, else the entry's name, no link label; a line whose own merchant the catalog knows keeps a «≈» link with the entry's name and, when only the bank's name decided its category and found none, the category of the entry's latest charge that has one. `replaceRecurringPendingLines` (`ccRecurringPendingLines.ts`, after every paste/feed import) deletes a bucket line under one pattern of an automatic UF entry once a line on the same card under another pattern lists the same pesos 0–7 days later fitting the entry at that day's UF (assignments carried, `recurring_pending` in the result; one-to-one only). Future UF everywhere comes from `ufProjection.ts` (`projectedUfClp`, `ufProjector`): last published UF grown at the trailing-12-month rate. Unpaid past due = overdue, counted in the current month. **`due_soon`** (`today` | `tomorrow`, Chile day, never overdue; set by `groupUpcomingByMonth`) draws a breathing blue dot between date and amount — full on the day, darker/fainter the day before; period 7,5 s ± 0,2 s per mount, still under reduced motion. Every API write drops the cached home bundle (not its account rows), since bill rows and declared payments are outside the aggregation funnels.
- **One cupo en cuotas rule, one purchase-cycle rule** (`ccCupoFrame` in `ccBillingViews.ts`, `billingMonthContainingPurchase`). Cupo is keyed on the open facturación, never the calendar month: the open month = live figure net of what a provisional close billed, plus pending cuota purchases; every other month = plan remainder after it. The detalle, `cc_billing_month_balances` and the month-end valuation fallback all read it: `recomputeCcBillingMonthBalances` writes rows, builds the detalle from them and copies each month's cupo and balance back, so `cupo_utilizado_clp` / `saldo_total_clp` equal the page's «cupo en cuotas» / «balance total». Facturado per currency; `saldo_total_usd` = the USD statement's printed deuda. The ledger's current month reads the Chile clock (`currentCalendarYm`; UTC is a month ahead from 21:00 on a month's last day). `billingMonthContainingPurchase` decides a purchase's facturación everywhere (first-cuota guess, feed-typed plans, web-paste nudge, `purchase_billing_month`, ledger facturado projection, mortgage UF reminder; cierre / first post-cierre day = `closeDateForBillingMonth` / `nextPeriodStartIsoForBillingMonth`). The config 21→20 cycle only estimates a close for a card with none on record and keys open buckets.
- **The detalle table shows the chart's month frame; the facturaciones table shows the statement frame** (`overlayBillingDetailMonthLines` in `creditCardChartSeries.ts`). STATEMENT frame: a closed month's cupo is the plan remainder after its close (facturado + cupo = balance). MONTH frame: the walks on the month's last day (today for the current month); a billed cuota leaves the plan debt only on its pay-by — so closed months differ by one billed cycle. The bundle's rows (`CcBillingDetailStatementRow`: `cupo_statement_clp`, `balance_statement_clp`) keep the statement frame for internal readers (anchors via `ccLedgerStatementClosingPointsClp` and `latestBillingDetailRow`, the `cc_billing_month_balances` snapshot, the «Cupo» card's used figure); the response assembly (`creditCardInstallmentsResponse`, `creditCardGroupLedgerResponse`) samples the lines once for the chart months and overlays `cupo_en_cuotas_clp` / `balance_total_clp` (`number | null`) from that map, so a table row («Deuda en cuotas») equals its chart point; group rows overlay from the group's own sample, never Σ members. **Never sample the lines inside `billingDetailCacheForAccount`**: the daily walk reads that bundle and anchors are written from it. Facturaciones have «Deuda en cuotas» (`deuda_en_cuotas_clp` = `ccCupoFrame(...).cupoAfterCloseClp(month)`, the statement's «saldo capital cuotas»; open month by the same rule; null without a plan ledger; summed in group merges). A statements-only card draws «saldo total» from the owed walk. **The open facturación has rows from the close day on:** `buildFacturacionesInner` emits it (`openFacturacionRow`: facturado = únicos + cuota a pagar via the detalle's open-month helper in `ccBillingViews.ts`, close from `closeDateForBillingMonth` with source, the «no row yet» pay-by rule, `deuda_en_cuotas_clp`) even without bucket lines, and `buildBillingDetailByMonth` adds `frame.openBillingMonth` (as-of today, `manual`) without waiting for the next `recomputeCcBillingMonthBalances`. Not for inactive cards. **The open row reads zeros, not «—»:** a cycle with no dollars billed yet has `facturado_usd` / `facturado_usd_clp` = 0 (closed months keep null = no dollar statement), and the proxy emits an aggregate for every facturación shown (`aggregateProxyByFacturacion(..., facturacionMonths)`: gain 0, `blended_return_pct` null when nothing floated → «$0»; «—» only on a card with no proxy). **«Compras activas» order is the server's** (`ccActivePurchaseOrder.ts`, in `ccInstallmentsDbApiPayload`): `remaining_installments` ascending, then `first_due_month` (none last), then label.

- **Feed cuota purchases, type-aware first-due nudge, feed mirror** (migration 186, `ccCuotaPurchaseKinds.ts`, `ccFeedCuotaPurchases.ts`, `ccFeedMirror.ts`). The Santander feed lists a cuota purchase at full principal, typed: «CUOTA COMERCIO» bills cuota 1 at the close AFTER the purchase cycle (that cycle prints 00/N); «N/CUOTAS PRECIO CONTADO» / «TRES CUOTAS … CONTADO» at the purchase cycle's own close; BCI bills «cuota comercio» in its own cycle (no feed). No count in the feed; a «cuota comercio» comes with a same-day stamp-tax row («IMPTO. DECRETO LEY 3475», same merchant) = principal × 0,066% × (cuotas + 1), capped at 0,8%, so `cuotaCountFromStampTax` reads counts up to 11. `santanderCardFeedPayload` (`ingest/src/santander/cardFeed.ts`) annotates typed rows (`cuota_purchase`: kind, count, source, tax; an unknown cuota-ish description throws; «CUOT: … OPER: …» references are not purchases). The import (1) **creates the plan** when the count is known and no plan covers it — `source = 'manual'` (so the statement's twin reconcile replaces it), provenance in `cc_feed_installment_plans`, `first_due_month` from type + real cycle (`billingMonthContainingPurchase`); it removes the one-shot line already on file and the feed row imports as an installment overlap; (2) **tags** the bucket line when the count is unknown (`cc_statement_lines.cuota_purchase_kind`; every precio contado and capped cuota comercio): the line stays (owed = full principal from purchase date), `facturadoClpFromOpenMonthStatementLines` excludes it from the billed estimate and `cupoEnCuotasForBillingMonth` counts it as installment debt (all «now» for the open month, the month's own for closed ones; a precio contado with the bank's SALDO INICIAL double-counts its first cuota until the statement's plan supersedes the line). `pendingCuotaPurchaseLines` (`ccBillingViews.ts`) is the one list readers use: the daily «deuda en cuotas» walk holds the tagged principal flat from its purchase date (so the monthly historial carries it), projected billing-detail rows add it every month so balance never sits under cuotas; when the plan arrives the line goes and the plan's purchase event takes over the same day. Expense rows carry `cuota_purchase_kind` (badge «En cuotas · ¿cuántas?»; dialog «¿Cuántas cuotas?»); the card payload lists `pending_cuota_purchases` for a notice above the facturaciones table; `convertStatementLineToInstallmentPurchase` pins a tagged line's first cuota by type. **Nudge:** `applyWebPasteInstallmentFirstDueNudges` pins a hand-entered plan's first cuota from the feed row's type when the row is its principal-sized purchase row, even over an earlier pin; untyped rows keep the open-month rule; plans with statement payments are never touched. The default guess (`purchaseFirstDueYm`, own cycle) is right for BCI and precio contado. **Feed mirror:** each fetch returns the whole unbilled list, so a current-cycle bucket line (on/after the feed's SALDO INICIAL close) that no feed row accounts for (same amount, matching merchant incl. the paste's 15-char cut, dates ≤ 4 days apart) was dropped by the bank: a restatement (a listed row of the same currency and amount under another name, or a matching merchant at another amount, within the window) is deleted with read-before-delete valuation re-sync; anything else is kept as an annulled purchase (see «Annulled card purchases») — only for currencies the listing proves complete (`feedMirrorCurrencies`, `mirror.proof`): its slide carried SALDO INICIAL, or the product summary's cupo utilizado equals the close on record (listing's, else latest `cc_feed_billing_closes`) plus every listed row to the cent (the peso identity holds only without outstanding cuota plans; a failed Dólares tab passes neither). Never payments/divisas abonos, never closed-cycle lines; >12 deletions on one card throws with the list.

- **Annulled card purchases are kept, never deleted** (migrations 332–333, `ccAnnulledPurchases.ts` table + `annulOpenBucketLines`, `ccAnnulledPurchasesRead.ts` leaf readers, `ccAnnulmentWriters.ts`, `ccAnnulmentCupoGap.ts`, `ccAnnulledPurchaseManual.ts`). A bank that voids an unbilled purchase drops it from «por facturar» instead of posting a credit (MercadoLibre 8x.xxx on ·7817, 2026-11: the cupo check read the app exactly that much above the bank). The bucket line becomes a `cc_annulled_purchases` row (amount, currency, merchant, purchase day, `annulled_on`, `basis`, the line's parser row id and flow; the expense key is rebuilt from those facts by `cards/oneShotLineKey.ts`, the rule the live line used, and never stored: keys live on flows) and the line is deleted in the same card-line write; a per-line category override becomes the «Único» row on that key. **Readers:** the owed walk adds it on the purchase day and removes it on `annulled_on` (`normalizedPostCloseLines`, keys `annulled|<id>|purchase|annulment`, no statement date); the cupo check's app side counts it only while purchase ≤ asOf < annulled_on; billing frames (facturado, cupo en cuotas, schedule, «Próximos pagos», the facturación modal) never see it. Expense lines: the purchase on its day under its original key (category, notes, big group survive through the retired flow; `annulled` on the DTO) and the annulment, a `card_refund` of the same amount in the same category (`annulled:<id>`, `annulment` on the DTO), so gastos nets 0; line ids `−(2·10⁹ + 2·id)` / one less; a category change on either line files under the purchase key. **Proofs (basis):** `feed_drop` — the mirror under its existing proofs; `cupo_gap` — both issuers: the bank's cupo minus the app's owed moved, since the latest earlier verdict that matched (else the recent one with the smallest difference), by exactly one unlisted open-cycle charge (none, several, a sum, or a close the app doesn't share write nothing and are reported); `statement_close` — the reconcile rule above, annulled on the close day; `manual` — «Anulada por el banco» in the line's ⋯ (`POST|DELETE /api/accounts/:id/annulled-purchases[/:annulId]`; undo only for manual, re-lists the line through `insertLines` with its key and flow). **Undone or settled:** a listing that shows the purchase again deletes the annulment before its line imports; a statement that bills it deletes the annulment and folds its flow into the statement line; a NOTA DE CREDITO of the same currency, amount and merchant within 180 days sets `bank_credit` (and `annulled_on` to the credit's day): the purchase stays, its synthetic refund goes (the nota is the refund). Not yet: a feed-created cuota plan whose purchase the bank drops (`cc_feed_installment_plans`) is not annulled.

- **A plan the bank prepaid** (migration 334, `cc_plan_prepayments`, `ccPlanPrepayments.ts`, `ccPlanPrepaymentsRead.ts`, `ccPlanPrepaymentLines.ts`). The feed lists «PREPAGO EN CUOTAS» (the decoder's structured `plan_prepayment`, a peso debit; any other cuota-ish type still throws) for a plan's whole principal, usually beside a «NOTA DE CREDITO» (`credit_note`) that refunds the purchase backdated to its day. `applyCardListing` pairs the prepayment with exactly ONE unbilled plan of the card (merchant stem, bought on/before the day, principal = the prepayment to the peso, no cuota billed) BEFORE any write — none or several throw and the document stays staged — and the credit with the same plan (amount, merchant, between purchase and payoff). The prepaid plan leaves the cuota schedule, `cuotas por facturar` and the cupo en cuotas (`prepaidPurchaseIds`, apart from nota-`cancelledPurchaseIds`: its purchase stays a purchase); the prepayment line is a normal bucket line that bills in the open facturación, and the owed walk carries a payoff event (−principal on `paid_on`, `normalizedInstallmentPurchaseEvents`; the plan-debt walk likewise) so the prepayment moves debt from plan to bill and is never new debt (the month-end anchors read the plan out of the remainder and the line in). Gastos: the prepayment line is dropped, the credit takes the plan's category and counts in «Total» only, so purchase and refund net to zero. A statement close keeps both lines unless a statement line matches them, and hands the pairing to the matching statement lines.
- **Same-statement twin installment purchases** (migration 187). Identical same-day cuota purchases print identical cuota lines, told apart only by `#dupN` dedupe keys. A plan is (loan identity, `cc_installment_purchases.twin_index`): `groupInstallmentLoanChains` (`ccInstallmentLedgerMerge.ts`) gives each row its occurrence among the loan's same-cuota rows on its statement (`#dupN` order), builds one chain per twin and links each payment to its own twin's line (Nth-to-Nth is exact since twins are indistinguishable). `installmentPurchaseLedgerDedupeKey` includes the twin index, so the duplicate-fingerprint guard, the «Total»-mode loader and the one-shot overlap matcher keep twins apart (hand-entered plans are twin 0). The set shares one `installment-h:` key (category, «Único», notes, financing links), «Total» keeps one total per twin (`installmentTotalSlotsByKey`), and display dedupe collapses the Nth identical cuota line only with the Nth rendering of that slot elsewhere (`sameStatementOccurrences`). `assertTwinPlansStartTogether` fails the merge when a twin's first statement payment differs from twin 0's. Backfill: `server/scripts/repair-cc-installment-twin-plans.ts` (report-first, `--apply`). Acceptance check: `npx tsx scripts/verify-cc-billed-cuotas.ts` (from `server/`) — each closed facturación's printed cuotas equal `cuota_a_pagar_clp`, nota-cancelled plans excepted.

- **A 00/N row stores its principal; an unmatched hand-entered plan fails the import.** The e-mailed Santander statement's section «4. INFORMACIÓN COMPRAS EN CUOTAS EN EL PERÍODO» (layout `wide_master_periodic_summary`) stores the principal as the amount and the cuota in the cuota field, like every installment layout — `reconcileManualInstallmentPurchasesForStatements` compares max(amount, cuota) with the plan total, so storing the cuota as amount would leave the manual plan beside the statement's and double-count. The plan builder takes the largest amount, payment rows read the cuota field, both operaciones reconciles skip the layout by name. The reconcile throws inside the merge transaction (nothing written) when a manual plan a batch statement covers stays unmatched while a statement plan carries its identity (same total and count, date ±2 days). Details in PARSERS.md «Credit-card PDFs».

- **A re-imported line keeps its flow** (`flows/cardLineAdoption.ts`, `docs/untangle-flow-identity.md` «Step 5 as built»). User state on a card line (category «Único» row, big group, note, pieces, bill link) hangs off its flow (`flows`, one per purchase), not the line id. `mergeCardStatements` records the account's lines before writing (`adoptReimportedCardLines`) and folds each vanished line's flow into its replacement: same parser row id, then identity (account, currency, date, amounts, cuota numbers, merchant letters and digits; twins in id order), then the one surviving copy of that identity. A target already holding a different value keeps it; unpaired lines are reported on the merge result. A re-parse splitting or merging printed lines hands identical assignments to every new line; a new identical line beside a kept one copies only category and big group. When the replacing line is known but its merchant differs, the plan carries it (`CardLinePlan.carry` → `adoptReplacingCardLines` in the delete step): a bucket line the statement settles (`reconcileOpenWebPasteAfterPdfClose`), a cut/pending re-listing dropped for its fuller twin (`removeTruncatedMerchantDuplicateLines`), a planted payment replaced by the bank's line. Two things stay carry rules, not flow state: per-line category overrides (keyed by line id; `snapshotLineCategories` / `restoreLineCategories`) and merchant rules — when no rule reaches the replacing line's name, the rule is added under it (the statement prints merchants differently, e.g. BCI's « (T)»). **Merchant rules match the statement's name too** (`merchantRuleCandidateKeys`, exact first): without the PDF's glued « COMPRAS P.A.T.», then its 15-character cut. The history repair `scripts/archive/repair-cc-expense-orphaned-line-keys.ts` is archived (it imports the deleted `ccExpenseLineRekey`).

## Ingest API (plan in `docs/ingest-split-plan.md`)

- **Two packages meet here.** `ingest/` (workspace `nw-tracker-ingest`) reads the outside world; the server applies what it sends and is the only DB writer. They share only **`server/contracts`** (`nw-tracker-contracts`): the envelope (`schema_version`, `feeder_id`, `source {channel, ref, label?, fetched_at?}`, `payload`), result / error bodies, the kind registry `INGEST_KINDS`, a typed client (`createIngestClient`, validates before sending) and a JSON Schema export (`npm run schema -w nw-tracker-contracts`). Neither package imports the other.
- **Kinds are canonical, never bank-shaped:** a kind says what the data means; bank decoding lives in the feeder. One `schema_version` per kind; a change old payloads would not satisfy bumps it. **A currency is any lowercase ISO 4217 code** (`currencyCodeSchema`, `server/contracts/src/formats/schemas.ts`); the server holds each code to `currencies` on receipt (`server/src/ingestCurrencies.ts`: `requireLoadedCurrency`; `pesosOrDollars` / `dollarsOnly` where a handler takes only those — card sub-balances, bank and broker cash, mirrors, wires): unknown → 422 `unknown_currency`, known but unsupported → 422 `unsupported_currency` (`IngestRefusal`, rolled back, never a 500). `payment.processor_receipts` keeps any code unchecked.
- **`POST /api/ingest/<kind>`** (`routes/ingest.ts`) checks envelope → version → payload (4xx `unknown_ingest_kind` / `unsupported_schema_version` / `invalid_envelope` / `invalid_payload`), then the **receiver** (`ingestReceiver.ts`) runs the kind's handler from `INGEST_HANDLERS` (`ingestHandlers.ts`; a kind without a handler is a compile error) in one IMMEDIATE transaction and answers `{status: applied | duplicate | conflict, kind, schema_version, retry, dry_run, message?, conflicts?, details?}`. Handlers return `{details, retry?}` and never pick a status. **Store once:** a kind declares `ref: document | read` (`defineIngestKind`); a `document` kind's ref + payload sha256 is kept in `ingest_documents` (migration 269) once applied with a final outcome — the same payload again answers `duplicate` with the first details (nothing runs), another payload under the ref `conflict`; a `read` kind runs every send. Rows a resend must restate identically go through `storeOnce` (field-by-field, collects every disagreement). **Conflict:** a handler throws `IngestConflict` (`{at: <table>:<key>, fields, message}` + details); the document rolls back, never a 500. **Dry run:** only the envelope's `dry_run` (`client.send(..., {dryRun})`; payloads carry no `apply`) — runs the handler and rolls back; process caches are bypassed inside a transaction (`dbInTransaction`) so nothing a dry run read outlives it. `retry: true` tells the feeder to keep the document staged; `rolledBack(fn)` is the same rollback for tests and report scripts. `GET /api/ingest/kinds` lists kinds.
- **Access (`ingestAuth.ts`; rule `ingestAccessRefusal` in `server/contracts/src/access.ts`, shared with the ingest service's `/runs` and `/parse`, as are `DEFAULT_SERVER_PORT` / `DEFAULT_INGEST_SERVICE_PORT`):** local connections only (socket address, never `req.ip`); `INGEST_TOKEN` requires `Authorization: Bearer`; `INGEST_ALLOW_REMOTE=1` (refused at boot without a token) lets a remote feeder in. `DEMO_MODE` refuses everything (`ingest_disabled`). Ingest bodies up to 32 MB (rest of the API 2 MB).
- **Unanswered requests are resent (`createIngestClient`, `server/contracts/src/client.ts`).** A connection-level failure is retried at 2 s, 8 s, 20 s (`DEFAULT_CONNECTION_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`; logged by `ingestClient()` in `ingest/src/serverApi.ts`), then thrown as `IngestConnectionError`, named by `describeIngestFailure`. An HTTP answer never retries. Resending is safe by contract (`duplicate`, tasks idempotent, a double report refused as `run_not_waiting`). The service's `reportWithRetry` adds a 15-minute loop for a server that stays down.
- **A Gmail session that loses its connection is rerun (`withImapSession`, `ingest/src/email/imapSession.ts`).** Every IMAP fetcher goes through it: a connection failure (`ECONNRESET`, imapflow `ETIMEOUT`, `NoConnection`; never an answer with a server code like `AUTHENTICATIONFAILED`) reruns the whole session after 15 s then 60 s; imapflow's `error` event has a listener so it cannot crash the process. Safe because each document is written and ledgered before the next is read, and read-only fetchers clear their list at session start.
- **Run protocol (switched on by `ingest/switch-schedule.sh to-server`).** The server decides WHEN and WHAT: `ingestRunScheduler.ts` ticks every 30 s on the wall clock (timers stall in sleep, the clock does not) and `decideIngestRun` (`ingestRunPolicy.ts`, pure) picks the nightly for the latest 22:00 Chile slot (answered by an `ingest_runs` row, a queued job, or any daily run after it; skipped when a daily run finished < 60 min ago), else the hourly for the latest :30 slot, and queues it (a run in flight makes it wait, never skip). An hourly may carry the Santander **balance poll** (`decideSantanderBalancePoll`): every hour unless the login is latched, the bank was tried < 35 min ago, or 22:00 is < 45 min away; input = the feeder's `santander_state` from the latest report. Requests go to `INGEST_URL` (default `http://127.0.0.1:3002`); a run not taken is `not_started` and its job returns to the queue (offered again 30 s later); a nightly badges «Ingest service unreachable» once per slot, only on the slot's second unreachable answer. **Idempotent per run id:** a retry carries the same id; the service answers 202 for the run it is running, `already_ran` (409) for one finished; the server reads both — and a «busy» naming its own run — as started, and accepts a report for a `not_started` run. **Recording:** `completeIngestRun` → `recordDailyRun` / `recordHourlyEmailRun` (a dry run or quiet hourly records nothing); a run whose job stops heartbeating for its 5-minute lease is `lost`, recorded as failed (3 h / 90 min timeouts only for pre-queue rows, `job_id` null). `ingest_runs` (migrations 200–201) is one row per (kind, slot); `GET /api/ingest/runs`. **The ingest service** (`ingest/src/service/`, LaunchAgent `com.user.nw-tracker-ingest`, GUI session) takes one run at a time (409 busy) with the only runners, `ingest/src/runner/` `runNightly` / `runHourly`; each step runs one ingest command (`npm run <script>` in `ingest/`, server tasks via `server-task`, never the root pass-throughs) and returns the `StepResult` the command wrote (`ingest/src/stepResult.ts`, `{staged, inserted, fetch, differs, read_brokers}` to the file in `NW_INGEST_STEP_RESULT`) — gates read structured results, never stdout. Logs `cfraser/daily-run.log` / `email-run.log` (hourly rotates at 5 MB). It may veto a requested bank fetch (latched login, bank tried < 35 min — `vetoed`, not an attempt), raises the desktop alert on a failed nightly, reports to `POST /api/ingest/runs/<id>/complete`. `INGEST_RUN_DRY=1` = every run dry. **Run now (`startManualIngestRun`, `POST /api/import-sync/ingest-runs`, «Corridas de banco y correo»):** queues an hourly or nightly as a job (slot = request instant, `not_before` now, reason `manual: …`; an already-queued run of that kind is moved up), carrying the extras `runRequestExtras` decides at START (the one rule for slot and manual runs: balance poll, AFP UNO, AFC, payslips, MetLife). It answers the slot it overlaps. Refused only where the scheduler is off (the primary must know the run id). `GET` same path lists in-flight, latest per kind, next slots; the page polls every 5 s during a run. **Enabling:** `INGEST_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=1` only in the INSTALLED server plist (set by the switch script, kept by `install-server-agent.sh`), never root `.env`; off whenever `BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED=0`. `switch-schedule.sh to-server` removes retired timed agents; `status` shows what is loaded.
- **The job queue (`docs/job-queue-plan.md`, migration 246, `jobQueue.ts`, `jobQueuePolicy.ts`, `jobQueueExecutor.ts`, `jobClaims.ts`).** Every long job of the primary (nightly, hourly, «Run now», deploy) is a `jobs` row; exclusion is the resources a job holds (`ingest-service`; `server-restart`, exclusive), never clock windows; each kind's priority, resources, lease, dispatch and run kind are one `JOB_KINDS` row. A job that cannot start waits and runs late, never dropped. **`pickNextJobs`** (pure): a running job silent past its lease (`heartbeat_at` or `claimed_at` + `lease_ms`) is `lost`; a queued job past `stale_after` is `superseded`; the rest by priority (deploy 100 > nightly 50 > hourly 40), then `not_before`, then age; a job starts when none of its resources is held; `server-restart` only when nothing runs, nothing starts while held, and once due nothing of lower priority starts (so hourlies cannot starve a deploy). **Coalescing:** at most one QUEUED job per `dedupe_key` (`ingest.nightly`, `ingest.hourly`, `deploy`; partial unique index); re-enqueue updates it (newest payload/reason, earliest `not_before`, newest `stale_after`). A running job is never modified. **Producers:** `ingestSchedulerTick`, `startManualIngestRun`, the deploy agent (`POST /api/jobs`). **Executor** (`startJobQueueExecutor`, `JOB_QUEUE_EXECUTOR_ENABLED`, default = the ingest scheduler's flag): 30 s tick plus one on every enqueue/completion (`notifyJobQueue`); writes off lost jobs (ingest → run `lost`, failed run; other → «Job lost: <kind>» notification), supersedes stale ones, dispatches ingest jobs (claim as `ingest-service`, write the `ingest_runs` row with `job_id` and extras, `POST /runs`; busy/silent → `releaseJob`, not an attempt). At start a SCHEDULED job a daily run already covered is superseded (a nightly with a daily run after its slot or < 60 min ago; an hourly whose slot predates the last daily run). **Workers:** the ingest service heartbeats `POST /api/jobs/<id>/heartbeat` each minute (lease 5 min) and its report completes the job (a report for a released `not_started` run completes it too); the deploy agent claims (`POST /api/jobs/claim`), heartbeats, completes (`POST /api/jobs/<id>/complete`; lease 20 min). Worker endpoints use the ingest access rules. **Page:** «Cola» (`GET /api/import-sync/jobs`, `JobQueuePanel`): running, queued with structured WHY (`resource` + holder, `exclusive`, `draining`, `not_before`; worded by the client), latest finished; Cancel (queued only; cancelled slot recorded `skipped`, not re-queued) and Retry (failed/lost ingest run → manual run). `scripts/replay-job-queue.ts` replays slots (`--deploy` instants) through the policy. `ingest_runs` stays the detail table.
- **`bank_account.movements` v2: the checking «últimos movimientos» xlsx and the dollar account's feed.** A partial listing: `{ account: {issuer, product: "checking", currency: clp | usd, number?}, movements: [{date, description, currency, amount (signed), document_no, wire_reference}], rejected_rows }`; `wire_reference` = the bank's reference on an incoming-wire credit. `ingest/src/santander/checkingMovements.ts` decodes the workbook (Fecha dd-mm-yyyy · Detalle · Cargo · Abono; a future date is the bank's posting day, kept); `import:checking-movements` (spawned by `import:cfraser-inbox`) sends each file, archives it under `cfraser/checking-ultimos-movimientos/imported/`, and fails the step after archiving when a row could not be read. Server: `applyBankAccountMovements` → `importCheckingPartialMovements` (`PartialBankMovement`, shared with the cuenta-vista paste) + a `checking_recent_xlsx` batch. **A row the bank dated after the fetch day is stored on the fetch day** (Chile date of the envelope's `source.fetched_at`; a peso listing without it is refused, 422 `missing_fetched_at`; the browser upload and the cuenta-vista paste pass the request time): the fetch day is the latest day the money could have moved, the bank's date is kept as its posting (`recordBankPosting` with basis `fetch`, migration 329), and the note key, cartola supersede, transfer-leg and mail matches keep reading the bank date. Rows on or before the fetch day are unchanged. Backfill of rows stored before: `npm run dump:checking-listings -w nw-tracker-ingest -- --out=<json>` (the archived workbooks with their mtimes) then `server/scripts/redate-forward-posted-checking-rows.ts --listings=<json> [--apply]` (report-first; the earliest listing showing a row is its fetch day; rows absorbed into transfers and UI uploads are reported, not guessed). **Uploads go through the service:** the server hands the file to `POST /parse/santander.checking_xlsx` (`requestFeederParse`, `ingestFeeder.ts`; `parse.ts`: 200 `{kind, schema_version, payload}` or 422 `not_this_format` / `unreadable`); `not_this_format` (a monthly cartola) falls to the server's cartola-xlsx path. Needs the service running (503 otherwise). `ingest/src/formats/chileanNumber.ts` is a copy of the server's, pinned by the same case table.
- **`card.payment_receipt` v1: Santander's card-payment receipt mails.** `{ issuer, paid_on, debt_currency: clp | usd, amount_clp, amount_usd, card_last4 }`; source ref = message id (keys the synthesized payment, so a resend never writes a second). Decoded by `ingest/src/santander/paymentReceipts.ts`; `import:santander-receipts` (spawned by the inbox pipeline) sends each and archives to `processed/` unless the server answers `ambiguous`. Server: `applyCardPaymentReceipt` → `applyPaymentReceipt` (re-date the next-workday debit, or synthesize the transfer + plant the card line). The overdue alarm is `check:synthetic-cc-payments`, run after the receipts on every pipeline run.
- **`broker.movements` v1: the Racional crawl.** One read = one crawl stamp in `cfraser/racional-movements/`: list rows (`movements-<stamp>.json`, decoded by `ingest/src/racional/movements.ts`) plus the dividends API record (`dividends-<stamp>.json`); either may be missing. `import:racional-movements` (`--apply`) sends staged reads oldest first; the server (`applyRacionalRead`, `racionalMovementsImport.ts`) plans them (day-sum dedupe, cash legs left for mirror pairs, a blocked row holds back the whole list, conflicts and ledger-less dividends reported as `problems`), writes, stores dividend breakdowns, moves its coverage. Ingest moves its crawl cursor when the list applied and archives to `imported/` when nothing is left to fix; otherwise the read stays staged. A row it cannot decode is never sent and fails the step. **A portfolio liquidation is booked from the row's detail view**, because the list cannot tell it from cash sent to a bank: it is one «Retiro» row (class `pending-withdrawal` until the money lands two days later, then `withdrawal`). The crawl opens every withdrawal row's detail (stages its `status`); `parseRacionalLiquidationDetail` reads «Montos vendidos» (ticker, price, pesos, shares), the «Además, se retiraron $X de lo que tenías de caja» line and the «Venta» step's day; `racionalRowToMovements` emits one `sell` per position plus a `portfolio_cash_withdrawal`. A detail without sold amounts stays the cash `withdrawal` (manual); a Retiro the crawl could not open is `incomplete` and blocks its read. The server plans the sell as `stock_sell` ETF → Racional CLP with units (`racionalEquityAccountId`: bare Bolsa symbol → `.SN`) and the caja pesos as a transfer caja → Racional CLP, deduped by the day-sum rule (re-reads and hand-booked liquidations are duplicates); an emptied caja ends the comisión nudge. The list key strips `pending-` (`racionalRowKindClass`; stored cursors compared via `normalizeRacionalListRowKey`); the list amount may differ from the printed sale; a key no longer found re-stages the window, absorbed by dedupe. `accountUsesEquityMtm` exempts `isClpCashAccount` as well as USD cash (a `stock_sell` into CLP cash must not make the wallet an equity account); `stockUnitsInflowByMonthEnd` is gated on it.
- **`broker.movements` for Fintual: the dollar wallet's cash interest, read live.** Alpaca's monthly sweep profit sharing has no mail (the Alpaca cartola prints it later; `brokerDividendStatementApply` only reports it). `import:fintual-wallet` (`ingest/src/fintual/walletInterest.ts`, `--apply`) asks Fintual's `/gql/` for `stocksWalletCashInterests` (operation `WalletMovements`) with `FINTUAL_COOKIE` and sends each credit as `broker.movements` broker `fintual` (contract accepts only `interest` movements and a dividends record from Fintual). The server (`fintualWalletInterestApply.ts`) books a missing credit as single-leg `savings_earnings` on Fintual USD on Fintual's day; same day + same cents = duplicate, different amount that day = problem, nothing written. Runs every hourly and in the nightly beside the Fintual mail imports, under `NW_TRACKER_FINTUAL_APPLY`. An expired cookie fails the step.
- **`broker.notifications` v1: Fintual and Racional money mails.** `ingest/src/brokers/brokerEmail.ts` classifies `cfraser/broker-emails/scan-*.json`, collapses copies by Message-ID and emits what each mail STATES (`kind`, `ticker`, `fund_name`, `goal_name`, `amount` / `gross_amount`, `units`, `price`, `clp_amount`, `paid_to` bank | broker_balance, subject as provenance). `import:fintual-emails` / `import:racional-emails` (`--apply`) send a broker's WHOLE staged set each run (idempotent; an unbookable notification retries by being resent). The server (`brokerNotificationsApply.ts`) runs `planFintualEmailBatch` / `planRacionalEmailMovements` and decides what the mail does not say: bookability (`brokerNotificationIsBookable` — a gross-only dividend never is) and whether the Racional crawl must open (`racionalFetchDecision`: nudges after the newest clean read in `broker_read_coverage`, plus the monthly comisión). `details` carry the plan, Fintual retiros synthesized from a mail whose bank credit never appeared (command exits 1) and the crawl decision, written to `cfraser/.broker-email-decision.json` (removed first, so a failed send never opens the browser). `check:broker-emails` is a local report. **Fintual deposits and dollar purchases:** «Recibimos tu depósito» (`deposit`) books checking → «Fintual CLP» (a bank debit already on file is left for a human to convert); «Compraste dólares» (`wallet_funded`) books Fintual CLP → Fintual USD as `fx_exchange` with the dollars as counter leg; a buy of a fund no account holds creates the stock account in the Fintual stocks' bucket. Wires to Fintual go through Fintual CLP; historical deposits stay checking → goal.
- **A broker adapter (`brokerAdapter.ts`).** Per broker: cash account by currency, the account holding a printed ticker (Racional's bare symbol → `.SN`), dividend ↔ `dividend_payout` distance (Fintual ±5 days, `BROKER_SETTLEMENT_WINDOW_DAYS`, the one Fintual settlement window; Racional ±2), where a deposit mail may already be in the ledger. Rules written once against it: `planDepositFromMail` (checking → broker pesos; same-pesos transfer in the lead days = duplicate, a plain checking debit in the debit window = for a human) and `pairDividendPayout`. Checking is found via `checkingAccountId`, never a legacy import key. `brokerPlanRow` is the one report row. **Two accounts may hold one ticker (`accountIdForEquityTicker(ticker, { brokerCashAccountId })`, `accountsWithEquityTickerAtBroker` in `accountEquityTicker.ts`):** among several holders the lookup keeps the one traded against the broker's cash account (`equityBrokerHoldings.ts` evidence); one holder resolves without it, none or several at that broker throws. Fintual passes Fintual USD for dividends and multi-holder buys.
- **`bank_account.statements` v1: Santander's monthly cartolas.** `{ account: {issuer, product: checking | demand_deposit}, apply, force_reimport, statements: [{document, period_month, period_from, period_to, opening_balance, closing_balance, month_closing_balances, movements: [{date, branch, description, document_no, amount}], skipped_rows, notes}], unreadable: [{document, error}] }`. Ingest reads the corriente's xlsx (`ingest/src/santander/checkingCartolaXlsx.ts`, saldo identity checked) and both accounts' PDFs (`ingest/python/parse-checking-cartola-pdfs.py`, `parse-cuenta-vista-cartola-pdfs.py`, `cartola_layout.py`); `import:checking-cartolas` / `import:cuenta-vista-cartolas` (`--dry-run`, `--force-reimport`, `--skip-pdf-parse`, `--only-pdf=`; corriente also `--xlsx-only`, `--only-xlsx=`, `--dir=`) send one payload per account; the inbox pipeline calls them after `organize:checking-cartola-xlsx`. Server: `applyBankAccountStatements` (`bankAccountStatementsApply.ts`) → `importCartolaList` + `finishCartolaImportRun` (period dedupe, phantom months, cuenta vista month split, anchor re-derivation).
