The public homepage can open a marketing modal (`div.modal-overlay[role=dialog]`) whose overlay swallows the click on «Ingresar»; `login.ts` sweeps the fraud banner and any dialog's own close button (`SELECTOR.closeModal` — never an arbitrary button) before the click and retries once after a blocked click. **The private site logs an idle session out:** the SPA overlays «vamos a cerrar tu sesión por inactividad — ¿Necesitas más tiempo?» with a 10-second countdown, and long waits do not count as activity; `sessionKeepAlive.ts` polls every 2 s beside the running step and clicks «Mantener sesión» (never «Cerrar sesión»), and the step runner re-logs-in when `isLoggedIn` is false before a step. Chrome is launched with `--hide-crash-restore-bubble` so the post-crash «Restore pages?» bubble never invites a human to restore old tabs into a scraper session. **When the scraper reports a closed page/browser, check the crash reports (`~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/`) before touching selectors.** **The card carousel is advanced by a swipe when its arrow does nothing (`swipeToNextCard` in `cards.ts`):** after an arrow click that produces no `consultaUltimosMovimientos` call within 5 s the step drags across the active tile and waits again; «neither the arrow nor a swipe produced a new movements call» is the stop. **The session waits for the call each view makes, never for networkidle (`docs/santander-session-trim-plan.md`)**, because the SPA's analytics traffic never goes idle. The login waits for the login button and then for a logged-in URL plus the landing's `cruceProductosOnline`; the card step reads whichever currency a card opens on, clicks the other tab once and never restores it (the carousel keeps the tab), and stops once every card of the product summary was visited (the swiper does not disable its arrow) — **and fails when a card the summary says owes money was never listed (`unlistedCardsWithDebt`)**: no file is written, so the next poll fetches again, instead of the cupo check failing on the bank's side; the statements step follows the same pattern after «facturados» resets to Pesos and reads the tab bar only once rendered; the checking step waits for the peso `transactions` call and the download button. **A rejected login never hangs the run:** `openSession` closes the context it launched when the login (or a relaunch) fails, `main.ts` force-exits ten seconds after the run is over if anything still holds the event loop (logged, so a leak stays visible instead of becoming a hang), and `login` polls for the rejection toast (`TEXT.loginRejected` — it renders in the top-level page beside the `#login-frame` iframe, not inside it) while the redirect is pending, failing with the bank's own words instead of a 90-second navigation timeout. **A failed login leaves evidence and a wrong clave latches logins off:** every login failure (including «login form never appeared after opening the panel») writes a screenshot plus the frame and page text under `cfraser/scraper-diagnostics/`, quotes the frame text in the error, and records whether the `#login-frame` iframe element existed and its src (the frame loads from the private-app host, so a stall there is indistinguishable from a panel that never opened). When the panel renders the bank's «No fue posible ingresar a tu banco en línea — Comprueba tu conexión a internet» card in place of the iframe (typical right after a wake from sleep, while the network is coming back), `login` clicks its «Volver a intentar» once and restarts the 45-second form wait (a second card fails with the bank's words), and `openSession` probes both login hosts before launching Chrome (`waitForHosts` in `ingest/src/network.ts`: any HTTP answer counts, up to 90 s, a wake younger than five minutes named in the log). The toast «Alguno de los datos ingresados es incorrecto» means the stored Clave Digital is wrong, and presenting it nightly is how a bank blocks a clave — so `loginLatch.ts` records the rejection in `cfraser/.santander-login-rejected.json` and `runSantander` refuses to open a browser while it stands, failing the step loudly with the fix in the message. The latch clears itself when the Keychain item's modification date is later than the rejection (`readKeychainItemModifiedAt` — attributes only, never `-w`), on a successful login, or for one run with `--force`.
- **The payment-mirror converter runs twice per nightly run**: inside the inbox pipeline (after the receipts step) and again after `import:santander-movements`, because the card's PAGO / ABONO DE DIVISAS evidence for a same-day payment only lands with the feed. A run with no step recorded is a failure (`runStepsSucceeded`).
- **The 22:00 run skips only right behind another run.** The scheduler supersedes the nightly when a daily run finished less than 60 minutes ago (`DAILY_RUN_REPEAT_WINDOW_MINUTES`, `ingestRunPolicy.ts`). Don't skip it on any earlier run that Chile day: an afternoon manual or wake-up run would cancel the evening's run and leave the afternoon's card movements for the next night.
- **Racional import: one read never blocks another (through `broker.movements`).** Rows missing units or instrument are `incomplete`, dedupe runs first, and only an incomplete row that would be written blocks its own read (nothing from its list written, the crawl cursor does not move past it, step exits 1; later reads and their dividends still apply). The crawl cursor is ingest's (`cfraser/.racional-crawl-cursor.json`, `crawlCursor.ts`): the list key (`racionalListRowKey`, the crawl's own `rowKey`) of the first row of the newest read whose list the server applied, forward-only. A Racional e-mail nudge retires once a read that started after the mail applied cleanly (`broker_read_coverage`, migration 202). Details in PARSERS.md «Racional (brokerage)».
- **The bank's own cupo is checked every night (migration 188, `santanderBankCupo.ts`, `ccOwedByCurrency.ts`, `ccBankCupoCheck.ts`).** Santander's landing page requests a product summary once per login (`cruceProductosOnline`); its `TCR` rows state each card's cupo / utilizado / disponible in CLP and in USD (18-digit strings with two implied decimals in both currencies). The fetcher keeps them in the card-movements file (`cupos`, or `cupos: null` + `cuposError` when the session got none — the movements step never fails over it). `import:santander-movements` validates them before writing anything (cupo = utilizado + disponible to the cent; bank account and plastic route to the same master; one row per master and currency) and records them after the lines (`cc_bank_cupo_captures` / `cc_bank_cupo_snapshots`, idempotent per file). The step «CC bank cupo check» (`check:cc-bank-cupo`, nightly after the payment-mirror pass and after the hourly poll's import) judges the latest capture once against the app's owed per currency (`ccOwedByCurrency`: `clp` and a `foreign` entry per currency the card owes in; each line in its own currency): the last closed facturación's billed total (statement header, or the feed's SALDO INICIAL on a provisional close) + plan cuotas not billed yet over every plan bought through that day (`installmentRemainderAfterFacturacionClp` — no calendar month-end cut, unlike `installmentRemainingClpByCalendarMonth`) + every line filed under a later facturación (charges +, payments and notas −, cuota lines left to the plans). That is the bank's own identity (utilizado = SALDO INICIAL + saldo capital cuotas + unbilled feed rows), so pesos and dollars agree exactly (tolerance 0): a plan's unbilled remainder is its principal (BCI: the cuota lines' printed «Monto operación», below N × cuota) less the cuotas already billed, and Santander's unprinted cuotas follow its own split (`CC_ISSUERS.cuota_split`: round half up, the last cuota absorbs); a card that stopped billing uses its last statement close; a snapshot whose feed states another close than the app's latest is `indeterminate`. The report with both sides and the app's terms is a **log** (import sync; verdicts in `cc_bank_cupo_checks`); a mismatch is a notification once per difference and does not fail the step (see «The hourly Santander balance poll»); a feed without a summary fails once. **A row the listing shows twice is two charges (`webPasteListingDedupeKeys`):** a pasted table or the nightly feed is the complete «movimientos no facturados» list, so identical rows in it are twins, never one row listed twice — the Nth one gets the parser's `#dupN` one-shot key and imports (the merge treats a fresh `#dup` key with a colliding computed key as an affirmed twin and skips the fuzzy match for it), re-pasting matches each twin on its own key, the feed close's move-forward and the mirror (one-to-one) see the twins too, and the statement's `#dup` lines settle them at the close. Don't collapse repeated paste rows: that leaves the app one charge short until the statement. The card page shows the bank's figures and the verdict under «Cupo» (`bank_cupo` on the card payload; the «Cupo» value itself mixes CLP with the dollars in pesos).
- **The bank's own deposit-account balances are checked nightly too (migration 212, `bankAccountBalances.ts`, kind `bank_account.balances`).** The same product summary lists the client's deposit accounts (`CCC` cuenta corriente in pesos and dollars, `CCM` cuenta vista; `MONTODISPONIBLE` is the balance). The fetcher keeps those rows in the card-movements file (`accounts`), `import:santander-movements` sends them after the card listing (same source ref), and the server records every row; only numbers declared in `bank_account_numbers` (account ↔ issuer + number + currency, set on the live DB — personal data) are judged, inside the «CC bank cupo check» step: the ledger in the account's own currency at the observation's Chile day (display readers) must equal the bank to the cent, each snapshot judged once, the report a log, a mismatch a notification once per difference. A row in any code of `currencies` is recorded and judged in the account's own currency to its coin — pesos against the peso checking ledger, any other against the cash ledger held in it (`ledgerBalanceInOwnCurrency`; a declared account held in another currency throws); the product summary asks for a listing only in the currencies the session reads (`CHECKING_LISTING_BY_CURRENCY`: pesos, dollars), any other mismatch is reported with no listing. The summary balance is live, so a post-cutoff wire is in it as soon as it happens — the same moment the ledger counts the forward-posted row.
- **Fail-soft, but never silent.** Steps do not abort the run as a group — one bank being down still lets the rest import — so the outcome is *recorded*, not just logged: `recordDailyRun` (`server/src/dailyRunLog.ts`, from the run report `completeIngestRun` receives) writes an `app_messages` row per run — a plain `log` when every step passed, a **`notification`** (which the app badges unread) plus a macOS alert from the service when any failed; a run whose job stops heartbeating is recorded as a failed run (`lost`). **The first clean run after a badged failure is a `notification` too** — under the clean title (the titles drive `lastDailyRunAt` / `staleDailyRunDays`; kind is read by none of them), with a «Recovered: the previous run (YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM) had failed.» line, because the notifications tab lists notifications only and a resolved outage would otherwise sit there unresolved. Same rule for the hourly poll, where the recovery records even when the poll was quiet (`lastRunFailureToRecoverFrom` in `dailyRunLog.ts`); the next clean run finds a success on top and is a plain log again. **A recovery is a notification only when its outage was badged:** the poll badges one failure per Chile day, so a second outage of the day is logs only and its recovery is a log too (recorded, with the «Recovered» line, so import sync shows the outage end).
- The message body names each step and its duration **and the days since the last successful run** — the failure a per-run status cannot express is the run that never happened (machine asleep at 22:00, agent unloaded). `staleDailyRunDays()` is the read side for any surface that wants to show it.

## Global sync scheduler

- In-process scheduler (`server/src/globalSyncScheduler.ts`): polls every 15 minutes **only while any source is stale**; when all are fresh, stops polling and wakes at the earliest source `next_sync` wall time.
- **One scheduler kernel, one source table, one config (`schedulerKernel.ts`, `globalSyncStale.ts` `SYNC_SOURCES`, `publisherSchedule.ts`, `config.ts`, `outboundFetch.ts`; docs/untangle-plan.md F14).** Every background job — the global sync, the live-quote poll and its two companions (the watchlist history depth, the sync dimming check), the ingest scheduler's 30 s tick, the job queue's executor, the warmer's 00:05 rollover and data-version check, the hourly backup check, the usage flush — is a `ScheduledJob`: it says when it next wants to run (a wall-clock instant) and what a run does; the kernel owns one timer per job, suspend detection (how late the timer fired, or the wall clock running ahead of the monotonic one), the network grace after a wake, the bounded retry budget refilled by a success (`every failure` for the live poll, `only while recovering from a wake` for the global sync), the in-flight guard and the enabled / awake gates. A sync source is one row of `SYNC_SOURCES` (label, configured, stale rule, publisher lag and its dimming hour, wake time, day kind, whether a forced run survives a still-stale success); the status rows, the scheduler's stale list, the dimming list and the step labels are read from it, and the ids live in the leaf `syncSourceIds.ts`. A business-day publisher is a `PublisherSchedule` (`{publishHour, lagBusinessDays}`): the SP (AFC, and the AFP read from the SP) lag 1, UNO's own API lag 2; the evening publish hour of Fintual, the AFP and the AFC is one constant, `EVENING_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE`, and the publisher-lag verdict is one function (`publisherLagFromPoll`). Every sync fetch goes through `fetchOutboundText` (user agent, a 60 s timeout unless stated, Latin-1 or UTF-8, Banco Central's rate gate).
- **Only the user's primary app instance runs background jobs.** Any secondary dev server sharing the real DB — every `.claude/launch.json` server config (already set) or an ad-hoc `npm run dev -w nw-tracker-server` — must set **`BACKGROUND_JOBS_ENABLED=0`**: two in-process schedulers on one SQLite file double-run every due sync (duplicate `Sync` rows in `app_messages`, identical diffs) and race writes ("database is locked"). See the env bullet under Server runtime modes.
- **Crypto EOD** (`crypto_eod`): due from 23:55 Chile; `equity_daily` trade dates are **UTC** days. Source: CoinGecko daily USD (includes weekends). Due close = last **completed** UTC day at sync time (never the in-progress UTC calendar day). Stale carryover persists after midnight Chile until that day is in DB (`cryptoEodDueUtcYmd` in `equityEodSync.ts`). Backfill: `npm run backfill:crypto-coingecko-eod -w nw-tracker-server`.
- Manual “Marcar desactualizado” calls `notifyGlobalSyncScheduler()` to start polling immediately.
- **IPC is the Banco Central's base-2023 spliced index, checked against its own monthly variation (`ipcSeries.ts`, `fetchIpcMonthsVerified` in `bcentralApi.ts`).** `ipc_daily` stores the index only (`G073.IPC.IND.2023.M`, first-of-month dates, 1998 →); every fetch also reads the published variation (`G073.IPC.VAR.2023.M`) and throws unless each month's index ratio matches it within 1e-5 points. Both print plain decimals and go through `parseUsNumber` — never the generic `parseBcentralNumber`, which reads «133.823492253» as 133.823.492.253. The sync re-fetches the last stored month and `writeVerifiedIpcRows` throws when a stored month differs (a revision or rebase is never spliced on); reload with `npm run backfill:sbif-utm-ipc -w nw-tracker-server -- --replace-ipc`. The splice is not the INE's official variation in every month (the UF, which moves with the official one-decimal variation, disagrees with the rounded spliced variation around base changes and rounding edges), so a tax reajuste that must follow the SII's figures cannot assume them equal.
- **The official IPC variation lives apart, in `ipc_official_monthly` (migration 196, `siiOfficialIpc.ts`).** The INE's monthly variation as the SII prints it (`sii.cl/valores_y_fechas/utm/utm<year>.htm`, one decimal) — what the UF follows and what the SII reajusta with — plus the index as printed in the base of its day (ratios across the 2019 and 2024 base changes are meaningless; reajustes chain the variations, `officialIpcVariationPctBetween`). Every month is checked against the UF (growth from the 9th of the next month to the 9th of the one after, rounded to one decimal); only the two latest months may wait for their UF, anything older throws, and a stored month whose figures change throws. Filled by `server/scripts/backfill-sii-official-ipc.ts` (from 2016, since `uf_daily` starts mid-2015); the monthly `sbif_ipc` sync step fetches this year's page (and last year's until its December is stored).
- **The AFP account's ledger and price (`afpUnoOfficialSync.ts`, `afpDisplayFrame.ts`, `pensionLedgerShape.ts`, `spAfpFundUnits.ts`, migration 206).** The price is the Superintendencia de Pensiones' official valor cuota (`vcfAFPxls.php`, one fund per file, every AFP, yearly blocks with their own header — UNO from 2019-10 — and a «Valores Provisorios» block that reprints the current month), stored with the SP's own date in `pension_fund_unit_official` (every AFP, fund A; a stored value the SP prints differently is replaced and noted). **`afp_uno_cuota_a` is the display frame:** the official value of Chile business day D shows from the next business day (`AFP_UNO_DISPLAY_LAG_BUSINESS_DAYS` = 1), the day it is published (the SP prints ~18:30–18:50; nothing changes at 00:00). The `afp_uno` sync is due from 18:00 Chile on each business day (`AFP_UNO_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE`, the same hour as Fintual so the two funds go stale together) until the previous business day's value is stored, re-reads the current year each run, re-derives the last 60 display days and writes rows only through the day the latest published value shows (a pending value is never pre-written as a carry); a late SP is publisher lag («Esperando publicación», dims from 18:00). No uno.cl homepage scrape and no Quetalmi (both wrote a 00:00 frame). **The ledger** is one row per day a certificate price first shows in that series (`pensionLedgerShape.ts`: rows netted per day, withdrawals their own row, provisions cancel; a price is looked for from six months before its período to eighteen after, since late cotizaciones can be credited much later), so cuotas enter on the day their price does and a contribution never reads as a gain or loss. **One account per fund manager and fund (migration 242, `afpAccounts.ts`, `pensionCertificateChain.ts`, `server/scripts/split-afp-ledger-into-fund-accounts.ts`; plan `docs/afp-fund-accounts-plan.md`):** «PlanVital-A», «Modelo-A» and «UNO-A» each hold the cuotas their certificate rows print, priced by their own fund's official valor cuota in the display frame — series `afp_<afp>_cuota_<fund>` (`afpDisplaySeriesKey`), declared on `accounts.fund_series_key`, which `cuotaLedgerSeriesKeyForAccount` reads (null means the kind's live series, `afp_uno_cuota_a`); `import_key` is `import:afp|afp=<afp>|fund=<fund>`. **A fund switch is a transfer between two fund accounts carrying both cuota counts:** `units_delta` the cuotas sold (the from-leg), `counter_units_delta` the cuotas bought (the to-leg; migration 242, read through `transferToLegUnits` / `transferFromLegUnits` in `movementTransfer.ts`, accepted by the create validation only on a transfer between two units accounts), `flow_kind = 'afp_fund_switch'`, `amount` the pesos the sale produced, dated the day the receiving fund's credit price shows; the sending fund's series ends on the day its sale price showed, so while the money is in transit the account keeps exactly the pesos that left (an on-or-before read), no phantom P/L couplet. When the receiving fund credits more than was sent, the difference shows as its P/L on its first day. **The live UNO account is the afp account whose series is `afp_uno_cuota_a`** (`liveAfpUnoAccountId`): the nightly reads, the sync, the stale rule and the `account_sync_sources` inference resolve it that way, never by import key. A cuota-ledger account with no stored `valuations` gets its chart dates from its units events (`augmentChartDatesForCuotaLedgerAccounts`); the position ticker is `<AFP>-<FUND>`. The 2027 generational fund is one more account, series and switch. The chain is built from the full-history certificate by `server/scripts/split-afp-ledger-into-fund-accounts.ts --cert=<rows.json> [--apply --live]` (report-first; input from `npm run parse:afp-uno-certificate -w nw-tracker-ingest -- --pdf= --out=`; the locating, pairing and per-fund net checks live in `pensionCertificateChain.ts`): each row's pesos must be its cuotas × its official price, each fund's rows plus the switch into it must net to the cuotas it sold, and Σ cuotas today must be unchanged.
- **AFC Fondo de Cesantía (CIC) is a cuota ledger priced by the Superintendencia de Pensiones' public daily valor cuota (`afcCicSeries.ts`, `cuotaLedgerAccounts.ts`).** Source **`afc_cic`** reads the SP's yearly CSV (`vcfAFCxls.php?aaaa=YYYY` — semicolon, Latin-1, Chilean numbers, one row per CALENDAR day with weekends/holidays as flat carries of the prior close, history since 2002, no auth; the parser fail-fasts on any header/row shape change) into `fund_unit_daily` series `afc_cic` (CIC column only — the FCS is the solidarity fund). The trailing month is «provisorio», so every run re-reads the whole current year (plus the previous one while its Dec 31 is missing) and **replaces** a differing stored value, reporting it as a step note («restated …») — the SP is the authority. **Due rule:** the SP publishes on Chile business days only: from **18:00 Chile** (`AFC_CIC_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE`, the same hour as Fintual's cuota and the AFP, so the three dim together) a business day's publication prints the previous business day's row, so the expected row is the business day before the latest publish day (`afcCicExpectedYmd`: Friday's row from Monday 18:00, Thursday's all weekend), and the schedule wake is the first business day whose publication expects a row beyond the latest in DB (`afcCicNextDueYmd`). Don't expect the business day before TODAY: every weekend would then wait on Friday's row and dim the AFC until Monday evening. **Publisher lag:** each fetch records its time and the CSV's last printed day (`afcCicLastCheckedAt` / `afcCicLastPublishedYmd` in the sync state); a stale row whose fetch is under 30 min old, whose DB holds that printed day and whose printed day is still before the expected one is the SP's lag (`afcCicPublisherLag`, the panel shows «Esperando publicación»), and it dims only from 18:00. Disabled when no account carries `fund_series_key = 'afc_cic'`; migration 182 rebuilt the CHECK-enumerated `account_sync_sources` with the value and declared the AFC account (`import:excel|key=afc`) on the series. **Valuation:** `isCuotaLedgerKindSlug` (afp | afc) covers every cuota-ledger branch — position meta (`getAccountPositionMeta`, ticker «AFC CIC»), historical marks (`accountMarkClpAtYmd`), today's live value (`accountTodayValue`: AFC reads its ledger BEFORE the stored rows — those are the pre-ledger benchmark, not a mark; AFP keeps stored-first because its sync stamps a today row from the same cuotas × px), chart live last points, perf cuota inflows, and the dashboard/detail overrides — with `cuotaLedgerSeriesKeyForAccount` resolving both from `accounts.fund_series_key` (an AFP account without one prices with `afp_uno_cuota_a`); an `afc` account WITHOUT a series key is not modeled in cuotas and keeps the plain stored-mark path (demo/CI). Manual AFC creates require `units_delta` (`movementUnitsPolicy`, unit «cuotas», reconciled against the series like AFP/Fintual), so the book-ledger edit is off for it. Full-history backfill: `npm run backfill:afc-cic -w nw-tracker-server`. The rates page has an «AFC CIC» chart (`market_display_series` builtin, rates only — the marquee renders a fixed set of fund chips).
- **The AFC ledger is rebuilt from the AFC documents, not derived (`afcCertImport.ts` on the server, ingest's `afc/documents.ts` reading the PDFs through `unemployment_fund.documents`; `npm run import:afc-cert -- --cert=<pdf> [--cartola=<pdf> …] [--replace-excel-rows] [--drop-ids=…] [--apply]`).** The AFC prints no cuotas anywhere, and none are needed: the CIC is valued at the SP valor cuota, each cotización is credited at the valor cuota of its **fecha de pago**, and the AFC's commission is deducted in cuotas (the one thing the SP series does not carry). So (1) the **certificado de cotizaciones previsionales acreditadas** (every cotización with período, empleador, monto, fecha de pago; two legs per período — trabajador 0,6 % + empleador 1,6 % — closed by a `TOTAL` line the parser must reproduce) becomes ONE movement per (período, pay date) with `units_delta = monto ÷ valor cuota(pay date)`, identity = note key `AFC cotización — período YYYY-MM (pago YYYY-MM-DD)` (idempotent re-imports; a changed printed amount is a `mismatch`, never overwritten); (2) withdrawals are priced at the valor cuota of their date, except one after which the account's own next stored valuation reads 0 with nothing else moving the ledger before it — that one **closes** with exactly the cuotas held; (3) each **estado cuatrimestral** yields a true-up per printed saldo: `units = saldo ÷ valor cuota(day) − ledger through the day`, `flow_kind = cash_fee` when the ledger sits above the saldo (the commission) or `savings_earnings` below — both are P/L to every deposit reader, never capital — refused when the period's cotizaciones in the ledger differ from the cartola's «Total de cotizaciones» (the certificate import is incomplete; a true-up must not absorb missing cotizaciones as «commission»), and closing withdrawals are re-measured after the true-ups. Without `--apply` the whole transaction is rolled back after printing, so the report IS the post-import ledger. The pesos ÷ valor cuota derivation script (`afc:derive-cuotas`) remains only as a benchmark tool for a ledger with no certificate.
- **Stale vs the publisher's lag.** `isFintualSyncStale` deliberately keeps the source stale while the cuota expected for a publish day has not appeared, so the scheduler keeps polling; `fintualPublisherLag` (`globalSyncStale.ts`) classifies a stale Fintual row as the PUBLISHER's lag when the last poll is recent (`FINTUAL_PUBLISHER_LAG_MAX_POLL_AGE_MS`, two scheduler intervals — read from the goals snapshot's `fetchedAt`), nobody forced a run, the API's latest published day is behind the first expected publish day, and every held fund's bar for that published day is in the DB; anything else (sig mismatch, unreconciled positions, a missed poll) stays plain stale. The status row carries `publisher_lag {expected_ymd, published_ymd, last_checked_at}`; the sync panel shows «Esperando publicación» with the two dates and the last check instead of «Desactualizado». **The dimming list is `stale_dimming`** (`staleDimmingSources`; `dashboardAccounts` dims from it, `stale` itself is unchanged): our own staleness always dims, a publisher-lag source dims from **18:00 Chile** (`FINTUAL_PUBLISH_HOUR_CHILE` in `fintualPublishDate.ts`, the one constant the stale rule, the schedule wake and the dim rule share) until the cuota is applied; before 18:00 — pre-open, the live proxy, the post-close hold — every Fintual account stays normal because the display holds all the publisher has. Wall-clock window by design: a cuota still missing at midnight reads normal again from 00:00 and dims at 18:00 the next day. **A cuota already in hand needs no same-day poll:** Fintual forward-publishes a holiday block's flat carries, so on a publish day whose cuota is already applied (`fintualLastAppliedPublishYmd >= today`, signature unchanged, positions reconciled) `isFintualSyncStale` is false without a poll that day. In `syncSourceSchedule.ts` «fintual» the next wake is the first publish day whose 18:00 is still ahead (today before 18:00, else from tomorrow — a block-closing Sunday counts) and skips past an applied day; never jump to the publish day after the next one, since the scheduler sleeps until the earliest `next_sync` when nothing is stale and would miss that 18:00. The applied day is threaded in explicitly (`SyncSourceScheduleOptions.fintualAppliedPublishYmd` from `syncSourceRow`), never read from the state file inside the schedule, so the schedule stays a pure function of its inputs.
- **A skipped Fintual day is a stand-in at the next value, healed from the public serie on any day (`fundUnitDaily.ts` `gapFill`, `fintual_official_serie`).** Fintual publishes day D's cuota the evening of D (the "D+1" rule is the public serie's), and over a weekend / holiday block a second value dated the block's LAST día inhábil, a few days of fee accrual below the last business day's. When the evening poll misses a day and the next poll finds only a later value, the gap days must not be copies of the PREVIOUS cuota (a carry would put the missed day's whole move into the next day's P/L). `upsertFundUnitSpotPreservingHistory` takes `gapFill: "stand_in_new"`: the Fintual path writes every skipped day at the NEW value as `fintual:stand-in|from=<observation day>` (a placeholder to `isFintualCarryForwardFundUnitNote`, never published, so the verifier replaces it unconditionally, the RN composition anchor skips it and the newest-bar check ignores it); other callers keep the carry (`fintual:carry-forward`). `fintual_official_serie` is its own sync source (the goals poll is not due on a weekend once the block value is applied): stale from 12:00 Chile, at most every 2 h (`fintualOfficialSerieLastCheckedAt`), while a mapped series holds a placeholder dated within the last 7 days and on or before yesterday; the goals poll's check stamps the same field so the two never run minutes apart. **Global sync waits for the network after a wake and retries (`schedulerKernel.ts`):** a suspend is measured from a late timer or wall-vs-monotonic clock divergence, a tick that follows one waits 10 s, and a failed post-wake sync is retried after 60 s and 180 s, the budget refilled by a success; a failure with no wake keeps the 15-minute poll.
- **Fintual valor cuota = Fintual's own share count, verified against the official serie.** The evening poll prices each goal's fund from the site's `/gql/` «latest accrued balance» queries (`clReserveLatestAccruedBalance` / `clApvAGoalLatestAccruedBalance` / `clApvBGoal…` / `clGoal…`, cookie-authed like `GET /api/goals`; builder + parser in `server/src/fintualRealAssetNav.ts`): `investmentClosureDate` is the publish day, `portfolioSharesBalance.sharesBreakdown[].sharesQuantity` is Fintual's share count (APV-A sums the user-owned and state-bonus portfolios), and valor cuota = ticker valuation ÷ those shares — **never ÷ the ledger's cuotas**, because the ledger lags Fintual whenever cash is in transit (a retiro sells cuotas days before the «Pagamos» mail lands the transfer on payday). The goals-API `nav` counts the payout (`reservePendingBalance.pendingPaymentWithdrawalsAmount`), so ledger cuotas × true price equals it by identity until payday, and `expectedGoalsApiNavClp` = shares + pending deposits + pending payouts is what a «mismatch» log line compares against. A goal with no Fintual price this poll (empty, or its closure day lags the publish day) writes **nothing** for its v2 account (`syncFintualFundUnitsFromResolutions` skips; no NAV ÷ cuotas inference for v2) and the missing-bar carry re-polls; a `/gql/` fetch or shape failure throws (never a guessed date or price); a goal holding several funds throws (one series per account). **Next-day verifier (`server/src/fintualPublicSeriePrice.ts`):** after every goals poll, `runFintualOfficialSeriesCheck` re-reads the last 14 days of the OFFICIAL public serie prices — `GET https://inversiones.fintual.com/api/managed_funds/serie/share_price?fund_serie=<id>&start_date&end_date`, no auth, history since inception, weekends included, publishes day D on D+1 so it can never serve the evening write — for every mapped series (`FINTUAL_PUBLIC_SERIE_BY_SERIES_KEY`: reserva2 → serie 1 Very Conservative Streep A, apv_a/apv_b → 7 Risky Norris APV, risky_norris → 6 Risky Norris A; ids from the fund-page bundle's `FINTUAL_MANAGED_FUNDS`): fills days no poll wrote (an EMPTY goal never publishes, so this keeps the RN-proxy anchor series current), replaces carry-forward placeholders, and **corrects** a published bar beyond `FINTUAL_PUBLIC_SERIE_TOLERANCE_REL` = 1e-4 — as a change line plus a **step error** («Errors:» in the sync log), because a correction means the evening derivation was wrong. Manual: `npm run fintual:backfill-cert-fund-units -w nw-tracker-server -- [--from=YYYY-MM-DD] [--series=…] [--apply]` (report-first). Provenance notes: `fintual:gql:shares-publish|<import notes>` (evening), `fintual:public-serie:publish|serie=<id>` (official), legacy `fintual:real_assets:publish`; `isFintualPublishedFundUnitNote` is the one predicate that protects them from carries and the unreconciled-inferred cleanup.

## Live market quotes (intraday)

- **Scheduler** (`server/src/liveMarketQuotesScheduler.ts`): polls Yahoo every **2 min** while a priced market is open and every **5 min** otherwise, on the wall clock (see «Polls follow the market clock»). HTTP handlers read **`live_market_quotes` only** — no on-demand Yahoo from client requests. The tick also runs `ensureWatchlistEquityHistoryDepth()` (watchlist.ts): `equity_daily` history to the watchlist YTD/YoY anchors (~400d) for watchlist + composite tickers, one Yahoo attempt per ticker per Chile day — `GET /api/watchlist` serves DB only and never fetches.
- **Prices come from Yahoo's price stream; the poll is the backstop (`yahooPriceStream.ts`, `yahooPricingData.ts`, `liveQuotesFlushPolicy.ts`).** The server (never the browser) keeps one socket to `wss://streamer.finance.yahoo.com/?version=2` (unofficial, no key) subscribed to the watchlist series keys plus every live exchange-rate pair's Yahoo symbol (`CLP=X`, `EURUSD=X`: `liveFxPairs`, see «Every core pair has a live rate»); frames are `{"type":"pricing","message":"<base64 protobuf PricingData>"}`, decoded by hand: field 1 id, 2 price (float32, rounded to the priceHint), 3 time (sint64 ms), 7 marketHours (plain enum — 6 and 7 are NOT zigzag: 0 pre, 1 regular, 2 post), 12 change (previous close = price − change; the stream sends no previous close for equities); every other field is skipped by wire type, and a frame without id / price / time throws and drops the socket. Only regular-session prints of today's session inside the readers' own gates are kept (`streamTickInSession`: NYSE / Santiago session, crypto always, an exchange rate while its fx day is open), so the post-close held print and the fx day end keep the poll's rules. **Write rule (user's choice): a flash is a real move** — each second, every unit whose price differs from what it last wrote and whose last write is ≥ 30 s old is written, several together when several moved, but never sooner than `LIVE_QUOTES_FLUSH_GAP_MS` (default 5 s; 15 s on the demo, `render.yaml`) after the previous write that moved something, since each one rebuilds the caches. A unit is a symbol, except the Risky Norris basket's ETFs, written together so the APV marks move once per interval. Writes go through `writeStreamQuotes` (`liveMarketQuotesSync.ts`): the poll's rows, change test, stamp and scoped invalidation. A symbol with a kept tick in the last 5 min is **covered**: the poll skips it (`liveStreamCoverage` → `syncAllLiveMarketQuotes(now, {skipTickers, skipLiveFx})`) and the stream re-stamps its row every 3 min at the same price (no change, no invalidation) so the 10-min freshness window never lapses on a quiet `.SN` or the dollar; anything uncovered (a closed market, a quiet symbol, a dead socket) is the poll's. While connected the poll keeps the 5-min cadence in session too (the open tick stays). Reconnects back off 2 / 10 / 30 / 60 s; 3 min with no frame while a subscribed market trades closes the socket. The client polls the stamp every 5 s and refetches by `X-Live-Inputs` (see «Polls follow the market clock»). The hosted demo connects only while awake. `LIVE_QUOTES_STREAM_ENABLED=0` = polling only; `LIVE_QUOTES_STREAM_URL` overrides the endpoint.
- **Wake and wholesale-failure handling (`schedulerKernel.ts`).** A poll that fires an interval or more late (or after the wall clock ran ahead of the monotonic one) waits 10 s before fetching, because right after a wake the network is not back and every request fails. A poll where NO equity request succeeded (`tickFailedWholesale` — partial failures are Yahoo throttling and never retry; the fx leg is not consulted because its EOD fallback reports ok) retries after 15 s then 45 s: the budget refills only when a poll succeeds, so a continuous outage costs two extra attempts in total, a success cancels a pending retry, and a thrown sync (a bug, not the network) never retries.
- **Post-close hold + the mark date's fx frame (`equityHeldSessionQuote` in `equityQuote.ts`).** Between a session's close and the EOD sync landing its bar the live gate (`shouldUseLiveEquityQuote`) is off, so a session that has closed for the day (`equitySessionClosedForDay` — NYSE after 16:05 New York, Santiago after 17:05 Chile, never crypto) and has no bar yet **holds the session's last stored print** in `live_market_quotes` (Yahoo's `regularMarketPrice` re-serves the official close after the bell, so the print IS the close; freshness is not required, the session match is). Without it every reader falls to the PREVIOUS bar and reads a 0 day P/L until the EOD run. One choke point, consumed by the quote resolver (marquee/watchlist), `equityBrokeragePositionMeta` (dashboard rows) and `computeEquityMtmClpDisplaySync` (today's marks). **Paired fx rule:** a today mark's fx is `fxForLiveMtm(markDate)` in every branch — live CLP=X while the mark date's fx day is open (until 17:05 New York), the stored close after — and `computeEquityMtmClp` reads `fxForLiveMtm(asOfYmd)` whatever the price source (a historical date never matches the live row's session, so it reads the stored row). Don't price today's bar at `fxRowOnOrBefore(today)`: between the NYSE close and the fx day end that is the previous day's close, which re-frames every USD holding by the day's fx move. The dashboard row's USD frame (`current_value_usd`) divides by the same `fxForLiveMtm`. Pre-open: the prior bar at the prior day's stored fx (USD cash floats from Chile midnight, equities from the open — a known asymmetry, left as is).
- **Polls follow the market clock; the client refetches when prices move (`nextLiveQuotesTickAtMs`, `liveQuotesStamp.ts`).** Ticks sit on wall-clock multiples of the cadence (not a `setInterval` from boot, which leaves minutes of each session's start on yesterday's close) — **`LIVE_QUOTES_SESSION_INTERVAL_MS` (default 2 min) while a market the watchlist prices is in its regular session** (NYSE, Santiago; crypto keeps the base), `LIVE_QUOTES_INTERVAL_MS` otherwise — and each open minute gets its own tick 20 s later (`OPEN_TICK_DELAY_MS`). **A moved price is rebuilt on demand, and the client refetches only what read it:** a write that moves a price (stream or poll) publishes the live-quotes stamp at once and logs what moved (`noteLiveQuotesChanged(at, {tickers, fx, dimming, all})`, `fx` the pair keys that moved, a 500-entry log); the warmer does not react to `live_tail` (that would rebuild mostly what nobody looks at), so the first request that needs a dropped response rebuilds it on top of the cached history. `GET /api/live-quotes/stamp?since=<stamp>` answers `{stamp, moves}` — the union of what moved after `since`, `all` when the log cannot tell (a `since` from before this process or before the log's oldest entry). Every GET API response carries **`X-Live-Inputs`** (`liveInputsHeader.ts`): the request runs in its own dependency node (`cacheDependencies.ts`; cache and live-tier hits link their entry's node, so a cached response names the same inputs as its build) and the header lists the tickers whose live quote it read plus one token per live rate (`$fx` = USD/CLP, `$fx:EUR_USD` any other pair) and `$dimming`, or `none`. The client keeps the inputs on each payload under a non-enumerable symbol (`client/src/liveInputs.ts`; `j()` attaches them, a default `structuralSharing` keeps them) and `useLiveQuotesStampInvalidation` (polled every 5 s while the tab is visible) invalidates only the queries whose inputs meet the moves; a payload with no inputs (a scalar, no header) refetches on every move. A response reads live prices only where it shows them: group-page line and row order is by **yesterday's close**, not today's value (`sortNavNodesByMarkDesc`, `orderGroupTabRowsBucketMajor` — an order read live would put every price into the sidebar and the flows lists; it holds for the day), and the account page bundle builds only its own dashboard row (`buildDashboardAccountRow`), not the whole listing. So the sidebar, messages, reminders, benchmark list and flows lists answer `none` and never refetch on a price move, and a stock or coin page refetches only for its own ticker, the dollar and the sync dimming. To see where a response reads live prices, record the stack of each `noteLive*Read` call while building it.
- **A tick drops only what read a moved price (`cacheDependencies.ts`; the store is `cacheRegistry.ts`, see `docs/cache-registry.md`).** Every aggregation-cache entry and live-tier memo builds inside a dependency node that records the live inputs it read — a ticker (the live-quote row readers and the gates that decide whether to read them: `shouldUseLiveEquityQuote`, `equityHeldSessionQuote`, `getLatestLiveEquityQuoteRow`) or a live exchange rate by its pair key (`noteLiveFxRead("USD_CLP")`: `shouldUseLiveFxQuote` / `shouldUseLivePairQuote`, the live fx row, the observado window average; `EUR_USD` the same way); a lookup links the entry's node into whatever is built around it, hit or miss. The poll passes what moved (`publishLiveInputChange` / `invalidateMarketDataAggregations("live_tail", moves)`): among the entries an unscoped tick drops, only those that read a moved price, or whose inputs are unknown, go. A poll that also wrote the day's `fx_daily` close drops everything a tick can reach. A new reader of `live_market_quotes` must report through those functions. **So must every switch decided by the clock or the sync state** — a session, a publish hour, a holiday hold, a settled cuota: it reports what it would read whatever it decides (e.g. the Risky Norris proxy gate `shouldUseRiskyNorrisProxyMtm` reports the basket and the dollar), or entries cached before the switch survive every tick and read 0 day P/L. Two inputs that are not prices: the dashboard's sync dimming (`stale_dimming`, turning at 18:00 Chile) is reported with `noteSyncDimmingRead` and dropped when the dimmed set changes (`syncDimmingWatch.ts`, checked on every live-quotes tick, which also publishes the refetch stamp); and a global sync run whose Fintual poll changes the sync-state fields valuation reads without writing a row clears the caches after saving (`fintualValuationStateFingerprint`). A process-lifetime memo of database state belongs in `dbStateMemo`, never a plain Map. **Inside a transaction the memo is the transaction's own:** an entry lives while the transaction does (`dbTransactionEpoch` in `db.ts` moves when an outermost transaction begins or ends, when any transaction function throws — its savepoint rolled back — and on every `exec`) and until the change log records a write of a logged table (`mark_input_changes`, which the triggers fill inside the transaction too; a write that changes no logged row keeps it). Without it, ingest handlers (which run inside the receiver's transaction) rebuild the nav-tree model on every expense-line build, costing minutes per request. Chart-inactive (`accountChartInactive`) reads no price at all: an account valued from units (shares, coins, cuotas), a dollar account or a peso ledger (checking, a broker's peso balance, a portfolio caja) is inactive when its ledger holds none today; only the other accounts (the property, the mortgage, stored marks) read their month closes. An empty position reads no quote either: today's mark of a stock with no shares, or a coin account with no coins, is its sold-out 0, and the stock's position (`equityBrokeragePositionMeta`) carries no mark, all from the quantity alone, so their pages do not drop on their ticker's tick. Parity: `liveTickIncrementalRebuild.test.ts`, `cacheDependencies.test.ts`.
- **Response caches beside the bundle (families in `cacheFamilies.ts`).** The card ledgers (`cc.ledger|`, per card and per group), an account page's bundle, a group page's valuation / tables / monthly P/L / Rentabilidad (`dashboard.page_bundle|account_detail|`, `…|group|`), the dashboard account rows (`…|account_rows|`, shared by the home bundle, the nav snapshot and the account bundle) and a group's daily-series response (`daily.series|response|group|`) are cached; every API write drops them too (`index.ts`, request kinds `api-write` / `machine`), since labels, notes and card config sit beside the logged tables. The group endpoints share one consolidation per (group, unit, account set) (`groupConsolidatedMonthlyRaw`). The card page payload carries no statements with their lines, stored balance snapshot, per-purchase proxy or per-purchase payment rows (the page draws none of them). The warmer rebuilds the pages the usage log says are opened, most visited first (`visitedPagesWarm.ts`).
- **Manual backfill:** `npm run live-quotes:sync -w nw-tracker-server`
- **Env:** `LIVE_QUOTES_SYNC_ENABLED` (default on), `LIVE_QUOTES_STREAM_ENABLED` (default on), `LIVE_QUOTES_FLUSH_GAP_MS` (default `5000`), `LIVE_QUOTES_INTERVAL_MS` (default `300000`), `LIVE_QUOTES_SESSION_INTERVAL_MS` (default `120000`, poll-only), `LIVE_QUOTES_RETENTION_HOURS` (default `48`), `LIVE_QUOTES_MAX_AGE_MS` (default `2 × INTERVAL_MS`).
- **EOD** (`stocks_nyse` / `crypto_eod` in global sync) writes `equity_daily` after the close; live quotes are for intraday MTM / marquee / current-month P/L.
- **Cache invalidation (F2; `docs/cache-registry.md` is the full account).** One store (`cacheRegistry.ts`), every key in a family declared once in `cacheFamilies.ts`, one write signal: triggers log every change of a logged table, from any connection, into `mark_input_changes` (`markInputTriggers.ts`: `mark` rows carry the account and raw date a historical mark reads, `write` rows any other change; a table in neither list fails `cacheRegistry.test.ts`). The registry applies the log before a cached read, after every API write and on the warmer's poll; mark rows trim the per-account mark series (`markSeriesStore.ts`) and each family drops what read the change. Market writers of this process add a notice (`invalidateMarketDataAggregations`), so same-connection sync writes, which do not bump `data_version`, still reach the caches; a value no table holds (the live dollar rate the expense lines priced unpaid dollars at) is a declared input (`dropFamiliesWhenInputChanged`). A new cache is a family plus a key builder built through `getAggregationCached` — no invalidation call anywhere; a new table is a migration adding its triggers.
- **Risky Norris proxy MTM is framed on the NYSE session day, never the Chile calendar day (`riskyNorrisProxyMtm.ts`).** The RN / APV Fintual accounts value today through the live/EOD basket proxy (`shouldUseRiskyNorrisProxyMtm`) from the NYSE open until the Fintual evening sync settles the cuota that reflects that session, then read the official cuota until the next open. "Settled" is asked about `nyseWallClock(now).ymd` (`fintualGlobalSyncSettledForChileDay`), and the Chile-holiday hold classifies that same session day: Chile runs 0–2 h ahead of New York depending on the two DST calendars, so between Chile midnight and New York midnight the Chile day has rolled while the settled session has not — asking "settled for Chile today" there re-arms the proxy for the gap. **The Chile-holiday hold keeps the held session's close until the catch-up cuota lands:** `inChileHolidayProxyHold` asks whether the session the proxy is holding (today's once open, else the previous NYSE session — Friday's on a weekend) fell on a Chile non-business day and whether the NEXT Chile business day's evening sync has settled; it is consulted before the trading-day gate, so a Friday holiday keeps Friday's close on screen through the weekend and Monday pre-open, then Monday's live session, then Monday's cuota settles it (instead of snapping back to Fintual's forward-published flat carries). A normal weekend after a Chile business day never holds. **The hold is per DATE, not just today:** `riskyNorrisProxyAppliesOnYmd(ymd)` applies the same test to a historical date (the NYSE session on/before it fell on a Chile non-business day and the next Chile business day's cuota has not settled), and `getAccountPositionMeta` then values that date through `riskyNorrisProxyCuotaForMtm(series, now, asOfYmd)`, which carries the value on the SESSION the date reflects (live session, else the held one — never the calendar day), so every day of a held block values identically and its «Día» P/L is 0 by construction; when the cuota settles the predicate turns off and the block reverts to the official bars — the settle is a Fintual apply, whose full `invalidateMarketDataAggregations` drops the `daily.marks|` cache those days sit in. **The per-date predicate keys on the sync state's last APPLIED publish day reaching the catch-up day, never on `fintualGlobalSyncSettledForChileDay`:** that check also asks whether TODAY's positions reconcile with the last poll's NAV (`fintualCertV2PollReconciled` multiplies CURRENT cuotas by the publish-day price), which is false for hours whenever cash is in transit, and would then value every historical Chile holiday through today's basket (whose ETFs may have no bars that far back — a 500 on every daily surface). **The proxy's fx leg is the dólar observado frame (`fxObservadoFrame.ts`)**, because Fintual values day D with day D's US closes and day D's CHILEAN INTERBANK fx — the Banco Central observado published on D+1 (`fx_daily_bcentral` is keyed by publication date), an average of trades in a market that closes 14:00 Chile — and Yahoo's 17:05-New-York CLP=X close tracks it worse (late-afternoon peso moves enter the proxy one day and leave the next). `proxyClpFromMeta` resolves BOTH fx legs (composition_date anchor and valuation day) through `observadoFrameFxForDay`: the first observado row dated after the day (a gap wider than 7 days throws); while that publication is pending (today, or yesterday before the 18:00 `sbif_usd` sync) the running average of the live CLP=X prints inside the 09:00–14:00 Chile window (≥3 prints), else the frame's most recent observation on an earlier day — yesterday's window estimate while its publication is pending too, else the last published observado (never carry the last PUBLISHED row past a pending estimate: it pairs today's basket with an interbank fx two sessions old); a head older than 7 days throws (stale sync). `anchor_fx_clp` on `watchlist_composite_meta` is informational (the frame value at sync time). **The composition anchor is the cuota's own session, never Fintual's positions date (`resolveCompositionAnchor` in `fintualRiskyNorrisComposition.ts`)**, because the basket's price base and the cuota must belong to the same session: the anchor day is the latest Chile business day on or before the positions date carrying a published (non-carry-forward) Risky Norris cuota, the APV cuota must exist on that exact day (an APV serie that exists but lacks it throws; only a serie absent altogether yields null), and `composition_date` IS that day, so `basketReturnForHoldings` / `observadoFrameFxForDay` resolve on the session the cuota embeds. **The sync refreshes serie 6 and serie 7 from the official public prices first** (`RN_COMPOSITION_REFRESH_SERIES_KEYS`, the same verifier the Fintual poll runs), since the empty RN goal never publishes in the evening poll, and runs at **18:30 Chile on Chile business days and the day after one** (`isFintualRnCompositionDueDay`; a Friday's cuota publishes on Saturday, a Sunday brings nothing). **Self-check on every run** (`compositionSelfCheck`): the previous anchor's EOD prediction for the new anchor day vs the official cuota, as a step note, and a step error beyond `RN_COMPOSITION_SELF_CHECK_ERROR_BP` = 40 bp. The anchor only sets the proxy's level; its day change cancels it in the ratio. `fxForLiveMtm` / `fx_daily` stay the frame for every USD cash/equity account — only the RN/APV proxy embeds the observado.
- **The proxy basket is kept per Fintual positions date (migration 238, `watchlistCompositeHistory.ts`).** `watchlist_composite_meta` / `_holdings` carry only the latest basket (delete + insert on every sync); `watchlist_composite_positions` (+ `_position_holdings`) holds one basket per (bucket, positions date — Fintual's «as of», the holdings' `synced_at`) in **Fintual's own tickers** (`holdingsAsStated`; the SPXS → SPY price mapping is pricing only — Fintual's SPXS is the Invesco S&P 500 UCITS ETF, the fund holds no real SPY), write-once through `recordCompositePositions`: a repeat statement is `unchanged`, the same positions date with other weights a `conflict` — the sync keeps the first statement, still re-anchors on the new one, and logs a step error; the sync's note names what entered and left against the latest recorded basket, and the change line compares holding counts. Nothing on a request path reads it. Older history recovered from DB snapshots by `server/scripts/backfill-rn-composition-history.ts` (report-first, `--apply`; `source = 'snapshot'`; opens each snapshot through the `sqlite3` CLI with `immutable=1`, because better-sqlite3 cannot open a WAL snapshot read-only). **Before the sync existed the record is the regulator's (migration 239, `cmfFundPortfolio.ts`, `server/scripts/import-cmf-rn-composition.ts`):** the CMF's monthly «Cartera de inversiones extranjeras» of every fondo mutuo (`ffm_cartera.php` → `ffm_download.php`, `cartera=EXTR`, file `FFM_INV_EXTR_YYYYMM.txt`, columns per Circular 1333; Risky Norris is RUN 9570 «NORRIS») lists each holding's nemotécnico («SPXS LN», «FTEC US») and its share of fund assets (the last column). The importer (`--fetch` downloads the months missing under `cfraser/cmf-ffm-cartera/`, report-first, `--apply`) records one `source = 'cmf'` basket per month-end — weights renormalized to the foreign-ETF sleeve, `etf_weight_sum` = the sleeve's share of assets — and leaves a date Fintual already stated alone. **The filing is also a nightly cross-check (`cmf_rn_portfolio` global-sync source, `cmfRnPortfolioSync.ts`):** from 09:00 Chile, once a day while the previous calendar month's filing is not on record (`cmfRnPortfolioLastMonth` / `cmfRnPortfolioLastCheckYmd` in the sync state; the first run reads the latest `cmf` month from the history), the sync asks the CMF for it, records it as a `cmf` basket (kept out when Fintual stated that very day) and saves the raw file under `cfraser/cmf-ffm-cartera/`, then compares it with the latest Fintual-stated basket of the fortnight before the month-end: a ticker one side lacks, or a weight more than 3 points apart (`CMF_RN_CROSS_CHECK_WEIGHT_ALARM`), is a step error, the gap otherwise a note. A month the CMF has not published yet is a note («not published yet»), retried the next day.
- **USD/CLP floats on the forex calendar (`forexDay.ts`).** Yahoo **`CLP=X`** is fetched by every live-quotes tick while the **fx day is open — a weekday before 17:05 New York**, US and Chilean holidays included — and every CLP↔USD reader for today goes through `fxForLiveMtm`, so USD holdings move with the rate from Chile midnight to the New York close. At the day end the first closed tick writes the day's close into **`fx_daily`** from the same quote the readers froze on (`catchUpFxDailyIfMissingDueSession` → `syncYahooFxUsdFromYahoo`; the `yahoo_fx_usd` source is the nightly backstop, due at the same instant with carry-over), and **rows are write-once** (`insertFxRowsIfMissing`): Yahoo's daily bars — whose close is the London-midnight open, not the day's last print, and which disagree with its intraday feed — only fill days never observed and never revise a row, so yesterday's P/L does not move overnight. **Weekends read Friday's close**: no Saturday/Sunday rows (Yahoo labels its Monday week-open bar by the New York date of the bar start, i.e. Sunday — the ingest skips weekend dates, because Sunday rows would put weekend news moves into Sunday-dated month-ends). Banco Central dólar observado is stored separately in **`fx_daily_bcentral`** (reference only; synced by `sbif_usd` from 18:00 Chile). Backfill (gap-filler, existing rows kept): `npm run backfill:yahoo-fx-usd -w nw-tracker-server`; `npm run backfill:sbif-fx-eur -w nw-tracker-server` (BCentral reference + EUR).
- **Every core pair has a live rate (migration 323, `fxLive.ts`, docs/any-currency-plan.md C6).** A live pair is USD/CLP plus every core currency `currencies` loads from a Yahoo pair (`yahoo_pair_symbol`: EUR/USD from `EURUSD=X`), listed by `liveFxPairs()` and named by its pair key `<BASE>_<QUOTE>` (`USD_CLP`, `EUR_USD`): its `live_market_quotes` symbol (kind `fx`, value = quote units per base unit), its dependency token (`noteLiveFxRead(key)`) and its `X-Live-Inputs` token (`$fx` for USD/CLP, `$fx:EUR_USD`). **One session for every pair: the forex day of `forexDay.ts`** (a weekday, Chile calendar, before 17:05 New York) — EUR/USD trades round the clock on weekdays, but the day the app values is the one the dollar's fx day frames, so a euro and a dollar figure built at the same instant read rates of the same day. The poll fetches each pair while it is open (`fetchYahooLivePairRate`; skipped when the stream covers it, `skipLiveFxPairs`), the stream subscribes to each pair's Yahoo symbol under the same gate and coverage / re-stamp rules (`liveStreamCoverage().fxPairs`), and the first closed tick writes a pair's day close into `fx_rates` from the last live rate of that session (`writeLivePairDayCloseIfMissing`, source `live:EURUSD=X`, write-once like `fx_daily`; dated the Chile day, which is the London day at 17:05 New York, so the London-midnight Yahoo bar of `fx_pairs` finds the day stored and keeps it; a session with no live row is left to that bar). A day close drops everything a tick can reach, as the `fx_daily` close does. `rates.ts` reads a pair's live rate for today exactly as USD/CLP's (`livePairRateForDay`, fresh row, session on or before the date, else the stored close); a FLOW of today converts at the stored rate (`convertFlow` passes `storedOnly`), as pesos → dollars at the stored buy rate does, so the daily series' past part (a history-tier value, which must read no live input) stays live-free in EUR. **EUR/CLP today:** Banco Central's `eur_daily` carries no quote of today until it is published, so while EUR/USD has a quote dated today (live, or the written close) today's EUR/CLP is EUR/USD × USD/CLP of today (a mid; reported as both pairs); without one it reads `eur_daily` as every past day does.

## Page grid and tiles (`docs/layout-grid-plan.md`, `styles/tokens.css`, `shell.css`, `page-grid.css`, `cards.css`)

- **The shell is two tracks; only the content carries the column grid.** `.layout--with-sidebar` is `var(--sidebar-w) minmax(0, 1fr)`: the sidebar a fixed 11.625rem (sized to the widest nav label), sticky, outside the columns, its own `--sidebar-bg` (a shade above `--bg`), no border. **Its rows (`AppSidebar.tsx`, `AppSidebar.module.css`):** text sits on one column per depth (`--sidebar-text-x` + depth × `--sidebar-indent`, 8px); every net-worth group with rows under it (one that shows a chevron; a sub-bucket shown as a leaf has none) carries a 6px square in its card pill's colour (`markerRgb`, `mutedPillBackgroundRgb`) in the margin left of its text, and «Gastos» the expenses favicon's red down-pointing triangle (`EXPENSES_FAVICON_SPEC` in `favicon.ts`, the one definition both read), drawn in the selected chart style (`RoughSwatchBox` / `RoughSwatchTriangleDown` through `SketchMarker`, every style but plain; imported directly, as `Pill` imports `PillScribble` — lazy-loading them paints flat marks and bare pills until the chunk arrives). The open page's row has an inset rounded highlight and the groups above it read bright; a group opens/closes from a chevron in the same margin, which replaces its square on hover or keyboard focus (a group without a square shows it while closed, and always on touch; on touch the square stays and is itself the button). No carets, rails, hyphens or accent bar. An account that holds nothing today (`accountChartInactive`, dollar cash included) is not in the nav tree. Content is capped at `--page-max` (105rem from 1440, left-aligned beyond). Tokens on `:root`: `--content-cols` (4 on a phone, **12 from 880** on both desktop tiers, so a tile's span never changes between them), `--gutter` (1rem, 1.5rem from **1440**), `--row-gap` (= gutter), `--row-unit` (1.5rem, one UI line), `--margin-inline` (0.75rem phone, 1rem small desktop, 1.5rem big), derived `--content-w` / `--col-w`, plus `--hairline`. Media queries cannot read custom properties, so the two tier lines are literals wherever a stylesheet needs them; 880 is also the tables' narrow line (`useIsNarrowViewport.ts`), so the app has one small-desktop line. `.layout-main` (track 2) reserves the ticker's strip at the top and keeps `overflow: auto`, so anything wider than the content scrolls inside the main column, not the page; a table never should — it shows its cards instead (see «UI tables — desktop/mobile sync»). `.layout-main>.content` is the column grid; every page's `<main>` is one child spanning it. The ticker: on a phone a full-viewport bar and on the small desktop a bar across the content's width, both with the content reserving its strip (`.layout-main` padding-top); on the big desktop it covers the content's last 8 columns (5–12) and the content starts at the top. The prefs dock's max-width derives from the tokens.
- **Whatever a route renders is on the grid by itself:** `page-grid.css` makes the content region's direct child (a page's `<main>`, or a layout's) a subgrid of the content columns (`row-gap: var(--row-gap)`), so a page root declares nothing. A container INSIDE a page that holds tiles of its own says so with `.page-grid` (same subgrid): a Loadable shell (`GroupInfoBase`, `AccountDetailSharedLayout`, the tax return), a page inside the flows or panel layout with its own root (groceries), a tile grouping sub-tiles. Each section is a **`PageGridTile`** (`components/layout/PageGridTile.tsx`: `<section data-tile=… class="page-tile">`) with its heading INSIDE it, so the row gap is the only space between sections — no outside margins (`.page-tile>:is(h2,h3,h4,.chart-panel-title-row):first-child { margin-top: 0 }`, `.chart-panel-title-row>:is(h2,h3,h4) { margin: 0 }`; no inline `marginTop` / `margin` / `fontSize` on sections). A `.chart-grid` is a subgrid where it is a page-grid child (two panels on six columns each from 880; `--full-line` all of them) and a plain 12-column grid with the same gutter elsewhere. Chart titles and surface controls use `.chart-panel-title-row`; control styles live in `styles/controls.css`. **Account pages are on the grid (`AccountDetailSharedLayout`):** the content shell is a page grid, the strip a `page-grid` tile, and every section a tile (position, charts as `chart-grid` tiles, returns, detalle, cartola months, each credit-card table, mortgage form / sheet / scenarios / prepayments, capability forms, flows); a section made of several tiles (the card's cuotas block, the Pasivos credit-card and mortgage sections, the depto ledger block) is a `page-grid` container with one dim marker, so the same components sit on the Pasivos group pages inside `GroupInfoBase`.
- **A card's title is a pill in its bucket's colour (`DashboardCardTitleRow` `colorRgb`):** the nav node's own `color_rgb` (the colour its chart lines use, set from the panel), drawn with the shared `Pill` on a muted version of it (`mutedPillBackgroundRgb`: lightness ×0.9, chroma ×0.8 in OKLCH); small, not uppercase, the text the pill's own colour with only its lightness moved (`tintedTextOnRgb`: dark on a light pill, light on a dark one). A card with no colour keeps plain text.
- **Card numbers flash on a change (trial; `numberChangeEffect.ts`, `AnimatedNumberFlow` `changeEffect`):** when a strip card's balance or breakdown line changes in the same display unit after a settled render, the digits switch at once and the cell flashes green or red by direction (`flashOnChange`): instant on, ~200 ms hold, 2 s fade; a new change restarts it. The four P/L figures and the summary cards' balances (the page's own total in row 1 and an account page's hero, `CompactEntityCard`) do not flash; they keep the rolling digits. A currency switch, or a dimmed placeholder giving way to live figures, keeps the rolling digits. `NUMBER_CHANGE_FLASH_ENABLED = false` brings the rolling digits back everywhere.
- **Cards have no chrome and touch** (no padding, background, border or radius, no inset yet — aligning card text with the container edge is the next step; `--card-radius` only skins popovers and the modal). A strip is `.cards-strip` (`PortfolioEntityCardsStrip`: one `.cards.cards--summary` block over one `.cards.cards--detail` block; the accounts table a sibling `.cards-strip__table`), a subgrid of the content columns; each `.cards` block is **four equal columns of its own** across the strip with no gap (not a subgrid of the page columns: the gutters would split across the cards and make the outer ones narrower, misaligning the rows). A row the cards do not fill closes with a line just outside its last card (`box-shadow`), on the pixel the next row's card draws its left border on; dividers are per-card borders stripped by a **fixed 4-up count** (`nth-child(4n+1)` no left border, `nth-child(n+5)` a top border; never auto-fill), the one line between the blocks the detail block's border-top. The summary row is the same 4-up block with two cells empty. Below 880 cards are 1-up, 2-up between 600 and 879. A bare `.cards` outside a strip (the flows periods, the mortgage / credit-card summary rows) is a plain 4-column grid with the same dividers. `.chart-box`, `.chart-box--pending` and `.table-wrap` have no chrome (the pending chart frame is the title row plus the reserved height).
- **The dashboard's big-desktop rail (≥1440):** `GroupInfoBase` takes `rail` (and `tileOrder`); with it the strip and `charts` (the net-worth chart) go into `.page-main` (columns 1–8) and the rail tiles into `.page-rail` (9–12, pulled back one gutter so the two stacks touch), two independent column stacks (never shared explicit rows: a tall rail table would open whitespace in the main stack); below the rail band the tiles take their own order and span from the layout table (`PageGridTile` `wide` → `--tile-order-l` / `--tile-span-l`, `dashboardTileWide`): flows chart | Δ by class, returns across, allocation | the detalle table, then the wide tables, then the export row and the accounts tree last, full width on every tier (the export row always right above the tree). Below 1440 the two containers are `display: contents` and the tiles are page-grid items ordered by `--tile-order` = `DASHBOARD_LAYOUT[id].s.y` (`client/src/dashboardLayout.ts`, the one table of tile rectangles per tier in react-grid-layout's own shape — `GRID` is its `Responsive` props; `dashboardLayout.test.ts` asserts reading order, spans and big-desktop placement). In the rail a `.cards` block is 2-up (a `.cards--list` block — «Próximos pagos» — stays one card per row there and at 600–879; a `.cards--pair` block — the «Flujos» periods — stays 2-up below 600 too) and a parallel table shows its mobile rendering by the rail's width (`@container rail (max-width: 40rem)`). The home «Flujos» section is two tiles: `DashboardFlowsPeriods` (rail) and `DashboardFlowsChart` (main), on the same react-query entry. On the small desktop (880–1439) the periods and «Próximos pagos» share a row, half the columns each (`SMALL_PAIRS` in `dashboardLayout.ts`, `PageGridTile` `smallSpan`; the periods' cards 2-up), and below the big desktop the tiles take their reading-order index (`dashboardTileOrder`), not a `y`. Group pages have no rail (one stack, same tiles). **The detalle tables are row lists, one rendering at every width (`PerfDetailRows`):** monthly / yearly (`MonthlyPerfDetailTable`) and daily (`DailyPerfDetailTable`) rows read by symbols, not column labels (each figure names itself on hover): flows by an icon each in the text colour — expenses a shopping bag, income two stacked banknotes, contributions a coin stack with a leaning coin (the piggy bank is savings, on the home «Flujos» panel) — with the signed amount (negatives in parentheses) giving the direction; the net-worth rows' income and expenses are `income` / `expenses` on `GET /api/groups/net_worth/consolidated-monthly?installment_mode=`, from the flows overview's months; an account's units keep a → / ← arrow, the result ▲ / ▼ with its % in the sign's colour, YTD and Σ quieter. While every row fits on one line the rows are subgrids of the list, one column per figure; below the list's breakpoint (a container query on the list's frame, `perf-rows-wide` 58rem for three flow slots, `perf-rows-narrow` 46rem otherwise — never a measurement: measuring the rendered rows after every render flips them on each refetch and makes Firefox flash the page scrollbar) a row is three lines: the period; the closing with the result beside it, YTD on the right; the flows (expenses, income, contribution), Σ on the right — on a phone YTD and Σ share a fourth line. The result's % is in parentheses. No desktop table beside mobile cards.

## Per-surface display controls

- **Período/Rango are per-surface, not global.** Every chart owns a compact Período (D/M/Y) + Rango control and every perf/flows period table a Período-only control (tables always cover FULL history and paginate at every grain). State: `client/src/surfaceDisplayPrefs.ts` — `useSurfacePrefs(surfaceId, defaultPeriod, defaultRange)` persists partial `{period, range}` JSON under `nw-tracker.surface.<pageKey>.<surfaceKey>` (missing field = caller default; per page instance; deliberately no cross-tab sync). UI: `components/ui/SurfaceControls.tsx` in `ChartPanelTitleRow`/heading rows. The global toolbar (`AppDisplayPreferencesBar`) carries the display unit only; the old `nw-tracker.metricsPeriod`/`.timeRange` keys are cleared at boot. Bullets elsewhere that say "toolbar Período/Rango" mean this per-surface pair.
- **Defaults**: home value charts + group/account/Pasivos valuation = Diario+3y; every P/L combo, flows chart and the CC charts = Mensual+3y (the CC ledger surfaces are independent controls — `CreditCardLedgerSurfaces.tsx`, one implementation for the card page (`cc.<id>.*`) and the Pasivos / credit-card group section (`liab.cc.<slug>.*`): `.historial` is D/M/Y + Rango and the `.detalle` table M/Y period only; the group pages get Diario through the daily-series CC block summed over the merged ledger's masters, see «CC per-day owed + daily extras»); tables = Mensual (+ full history); proportional charts = Mensual+Todo (the Pasivos root's coverage chart, which takes that slot, Mensual+3y); rates = range-only 1y. **A Rango of N days is N days ending today, today included, on both sides:** `timeRangeCutoffYmd` = today − (N − 1), the same as the server's day windows (daily series points, flows overview day rows). Every client clip goes through `clipPointsToTimeRange` / `clipRowsToTimeRange` (no inline `>= cutoff` filters).
- **Axes.** Every value chart's Y axis starts at 0 for non-negative data and spans 0 for mixed-sign data — one helper, `buildNiceYAxis` (`client/src/components/charts/chartLayout.ts`). Two deliberate exceptions: the Rates page keeps `buildNiceYAxisPositiveBand` (a quote series is about the move; a 0-anchored UF axis is a flat line) and the wealth-percentile top-% axis is a reversed log scale that cannot contain 0. X ticks are **phased on January**: month strides are divisors of 12 (1/2/3/4/6) walked from the first month index that is a multiple of the stride, so a 4-month stride reads ene/may/sep whatever month the series starts in; the day-grid helper phases its month-first ticks the same way, and multi-year strides land on round years (even years for 2, 2015/2020/2025 for 5). Only the first-data-point push (year branch) and the appended last point may sit off-phase. Known residue: on a daily grid whose first month is partial, a tick that phases onto that month sits on the first grid day, not the 1st (`computeRegularMonthXAxisTicks` cannot tell a daily grid from a month-end series with a live last point).
- **Cards show all three periods at once** (no card period anywhere): metrics grid = Mes and Año deposits + P/L rows, a divider, then the dimmed Total row (lifetime deposits + P/L); the Día P/L sits on the balance row beside the main value, with no day deposits figure (`DashboardCardGroupMetrics.tsx`); then breakdown. There is no title balance-Δ chip / `title_delta`. Chart-inactive visibility = any-period activity.
- **Composition charts are proportional** (100%-stacked areas at every grain, own controls; no pies): server-built share series in `server/src/proportionalSeries.ts` — per date base = Σ max(value, 0) over member lines (negatives floor to 0, base 0 → null; Σ shares == 1) — emitted pre-clip as `group_allocation_proportional` + `nav_grouped_proportional`/`liab_grouped_proportional` (group payloads), `allocation_proportional` (home bundle + overview-daily) and `proportional`/`grouped_proportional` (`/api/daily-series`). Client renders via `ProportionalAreaChart` (shares are unit-invariant — keep-prev passes them through unscaled). **One level only:** the home chart stacks one level below the «big» buckets of the net_worth nav tree (`compositionBigBuckets`, `dashboardComposition.ts`: net_worth's children, where a `nav_bucket` without a `dashboard_bucket_slug` — Inversiones — is replaced by its own children; liability and reference groups skipped). Its lines are each big bucket's first-level children — a child group is one line (Σ of its `listAccountsForGroupTab` accounts), a direct account one line (name, account colour) — in nav order; label, i18n key and `color_rgb` come from the nav node and the client colours by the payload colour. Values are the dashboard's marks: monthly `slugMarkTotalsAtDatesClp` at the chart dates only (reusing the primary-lines totals for the same group), daily `accountMarkClpSeriesOnGrid` — so they agree at month-ends — computed in CLP since shares are unit-invariant. The linked credit-card netting (`cashNetOfLinkedCreditCards`) is subtracted from the savings line (`isCashSavingsNavNode`, same as the value map); the builder throws unless exactly one line carries it, if an account sits in two lines, or if a bucket account is in none. A group page's chart is always its direct children (`nav_grouped_proportional` is that single block) whatever the Agrupado toggle says, a leaf page its accounts; there is no `ungrouped` proportional block. Pasivos is separate (unchanged by this rule).
- **Value map («Mapa», `navValueMap.ts`).** A finviz-style treemap in the composition chart's slot on the dashboard and on every net_worth group page with something to tile — sub-groups or accounts of its own (leaf pages too, even with a single account; Pasivos keeps its chart); a «Composición / Mapa» select in the panel title row (stored per surface as `view` beside `<pageKey>.proportional`; the map's own Día/Mes/Año return window is `<pageKey>.map`, default Día; Rango does not apply). The server emits ONE tree, `value_map` (+ `value_map_color_bounds`), beside `card_metrics_by_slug` in the page bundle, nav-context and nav-snapshot payloads; the client picks the page's subtree by slug (`valueMapRootForPage`). Nesting is the net_worth nav tree: a group with GROUP children is a frame (header strip with name + %, when there is room), a group without is the smallest tile, which carries its accounts (`leaf_accounts`): a leaf group opens into them at any depth when it holds two or more, a single account stays the group's own tile except on the page's own first level, which always opens — the page root opens into its own accounts even with one; the client decides by depth from the page root. A mixed group (stocks beside sub-buckets) shows its own accounts as tiles beside the sub-group tiles, and those sub-groups are leaves exactly as the sidebar shows them (`mapNode` / `asLeaf` in `sidebarNavFromApi.ts`): one block, no `leaf_accounts`. Liability and reference groups are not in the map. **Additivity:** tile values are Σ of account rows' current values, each account claimed by one tile (a duplicate throws), so frames add up; the one non-row figure is the linked credit-card total, which the cash card nets (`cashNetOfLinkedCreditCards`) and whose footer sits on the savings card, so the SAVINGS tile carries it (checking is a sibling leaf group and stays raw) and the cash frame equals the cash card value. Every node carries its TRUE value, negatives included (a frame = Σ of all its children); a node worth ≤ 0 is left out of the payload but still counts in every ancestor (so a card bigger than savings + checking hides the whole cash bucket, and the root stays net worth). The client sizes a frame's area by its visible children; tooltips show the true value. The map shows whenever it is the selected view: during a unit switch or a refetch it keeps drawing the last data it had (`useHeldValueMap`), never swapping back to the composition chart. Size = value in the display unit; colour = the node's flow-adjusted % for the period (`row_pct` rule over the node's rows, a single row for an account tile; an empty member with no delta does not blank a group's %) against FIXED per-period bounds the server emits (day ±3%, month ±8%, year ±25%; null % = neutral). The layout is our own squarified treemap (`treemapLayout.ts`) — Recharts' `Treemap` cannot reserve a frame header — drawn by `ValueTreemap.tsx` with CSS tokens `--treemap-*`. Every frame keeps its header: a frame squarified smaller than its minimum (header + a body, and for a frame holding frames, room for their minimum too) is grown at its siblings' expense, and each node's area is its value at one global px-per-value plus the area its headers and padding take (re-measured over a few passes), so a tile inside a frame has the same area per value as one outside. A tile's label shows only when it fits whole (text measured on hidden spans): name over % from ~36 px tall, one row from ~22 px, else none; a header drops its % first, then shortens the name. The tooltip always shows value, % and P/L; a click goes to the node's route.
